Bheshajavacharaneeyam (Pre-requisites of
Treatment)
—_e eee snsesntsssesesessnnasenetensnesunsanoenmmnesennenen,

Points

* Rogi Pariksha (Examination of the patient)

¢ Dosha Pariksha (Determination of Doshas)

¢ Deha Desha Pariksha (Determination of seats of diseases)

+ Aushadha Pariksha (Determination of the drug)

e Kala Aushadha Sambandha (Relationship of time and treatments)

e Bhaishajya Sevana Kala (Time of administration)

e Dosha Nirharana Kala (Time for elimination of doshas)
Introduction:

Treating a disease involves several key points and when applied properly will bring
the best result. All these key areas are very well explained in the chapter
Bheshajavacharaneeya Adhyaya of Ashtanga Sangraha Sutra Sthana, Chapter 23. For a
successful physician the knowledge regarding the different useful points which are ex-
Plained in this chapter is becomes must. The Chapter includes several important pre req-
uisites to carry out treatment like Rogi Pariksha (Examination of Patient), Dosha Pariksha
(Assessment of Dosha), Deha Pariksha (Determination of disease), Aushadha Pariksha
(Evaluation of drug / medicine), Bhaishyajya Sevana Kala (time of administration of

medicine) etc. which are very much required. Before selecting particular drug / medicinal
plant knowledge regarding Bheshajavacharana is essential.

Acted WIT 1 (37.4.Y, 23/2)

Rogil Par pecs oe is very amnpOREE to plan the treatment so, Berane starting of the treat-

e In which region is he born? Where as he been brought up? Where has he
developed the disease? Which are the type of food, activities, strength, mental]
attitude, accustomed things, the likes, prevalent diseases, the suitable and
unsuitable things of the people living in that region? Knowing this beforehand
will facilitate determination of the specific causes for the increase of the
Doshas. Though the dosha may be one, there are many causes for its increase.
Carefully noting the qualities and functions of each, the physician has to
determine the dosha involved. For example, has Vata undergone inctease by
indulgence in bad food or bad activities? Whether it is due to indulgence i in
foods which are dry, light, or cold or by overexertion, suppression of the urges
of the body, fear or grief? _

Prescribing the opposite kind of treatment after determining all these facts, will
certainly leads to quick success. Sweet, Sour and salt tastes have their opposites
in pungent, bitter and astringent tastes tespectively.

Dosha Pariksha (Determination of Doshas)

It is to be ascertained that in what manner the person has indulged in the causes of
the diseases, whether by mild, moderate or profound measure (quantity, degree). Even
though the cause may be one only, by its different variations (quality, quantity) it pro-
duces changes in the different facets (parts) of the doshas which in turn make the disease
to become either mild or severe.

In diseases which have many doshas and in drugs which have many tastes, the
physician should carefully consider the special effects of the each of the doshas and of
each of the tastes and plan out the appropriate mode of treatment of the disease.

But this need not apply in all cases.

Deha Desha Pariksha (Determination of seats of diseases):

Further it is to be decided, in which place (part) of the body the disease is localized.
The doshas which have increased may not produce the diseases in their own seats but
may do so in other places such as the head etc. and so may become difficult to treat.
Hence the treatment has to be planned as is found appropriate to the seat of the disease.

Aushadha Pariksha (Determination of the drug)

Waves | acarsadteeres sarge at ates: Rerercit ar 1 ara fe
deerdemnfracdt enirentrraentet safer u (ara. % 23/4)

Teta dieses at 1 aarsttaen-

art enftraftaes siteraraeta 1 wtkaentrarh wtaestrenieacear |
aftaacrattert terrae (a8. F 23/5)

Selecting suitable and genuine drug is very much necessary to have best therapeutic
effects, hence Aushadha Pariksha (Assessment or determination of drug/medicine) be-
comes inevitable. :

To which drug and to what dose of it, are the disease and the patient suitable? Deter-
mination of the drug, its potency, dose etc., should be appropriate to the dosha, the strength
of the disease and the diseased (person). Whether the urgent use of powerful purificatory
drugs (or treatments) having the properties of Agni (Teja Bhuta) and Vayu bhuta is needed
or use of the drugs possessing mild properties in greater doses will be necessary, treatment
such as branding with the fire, use of alkalies and surgical instruments, are more harmful,
to patients who have poor mental stamina and physical strength. Palliative drugs (or treat-
ment) also if stronger than the strength of the disease will produce opposite diseases quickly,
after mitigating the earlier ones, in the body which has become weak by the effect of
disease. Those (drugs and treatment) which are stronger than the body will cause exhaus-
tion, fainting, toxicity, delusion and decrease of strength; those which are more powerful
than the digestive capacity will produce exhaustion and poor digestion. ;

4 Dravyaguna Vijnana

dean nang annealed atnarieanet 1

(Ha. Y 23/6)

Persons who are stout, very emaciated, weak, which have their body part not having
well knit muscles, blood and bones; of poor digestive ability, take very little food, who are
habituated to unhealthy unaccustomed foods; whose body is impoverished of essences of
the tissues, will be incapable of withstanding the disease itself. Then how can they with-
stand the effect of such strong drugs (or treatment) Hence, such weak patient are to be
treated specially with drugs which do not cause discomfort, which are mild, pleasant,
successively hard (increasingly strong) and which do not cause debility.

This should be so, especially for women because they are unsteady, tender and of
weak heart (mind), tender in body and dependent on others (men);it should be still more
so especially for children. _

Likewise, drugs (or ee which are very mild and of very little quantity (dosha)
will do not any good for severe diseases and for strong persons; on the other hand, they
will aggravate the disease by exciting the doshas.

eA ce a
santcaeret ova «offer: 1 at wa wa aete eos

Perea eS (3a. FY 23/7)

Though a drug is found suitable, still it should be examined to determine its taste,
potency, taste after digestion, qualities nature of the substance (mass), actions, special
effects, place of growth, season of collection, method of preservation, correct mode of
using, prohibited mode of use, methods of processing, combinations (or admixtures),
procedure of use, dosage, the type of patient for whom it is meant, the time (season) of
use, the dosha which it expels out or mitigates inside etc. if there happens to be another
drug of similar properties, then both are to be compared and the best of them should be
used.

Very subtle are the variations in different stages of the doshas, drugs, dushya (tissues

----- PAGE 13 -----
Bheshajavacharaneeyam (Pre-requisites of Treatment) 5

and wastes) regions, time, strength, digestive activity, food, tastes, accustoms, mental
makeup, constitution, age etc., which if not considered deeply will lead to death of the
patient. If taken up for careful consideration, these will create confusion even in the mind
of a very intelligent physician. What to say then one of poor intellect?

Hence the physician should study the scriptures, engage himself in practical work
constantly and transform his intellect for gaining mastery.

There are some diseases which stand out as exceptions to the general line of treat-
ment prescribed in the texts. In such cases, the intelligent physician should himself be the
judge to determine the strength or weakness of the doshas without seeking help from
elsewhere.

Kala Aushadha Sambandha (Relationship of time and treatments)

aves NSH AAAS 1A aToTagentfedeargquanan wo fear: were
a | vittteratoentafie: are: 1 aa vidteueddftesitaatrarsa aremuit
weoacraal | tiaatareateg, ureitge: prefer fracad | ay Aremraraey
amd vfafatafatarmstramrar 1 meron & wepidrradctargecand,
qaratrncrar: wreireerany | ferattarhearay ul (31.4. J 23/8)

Kala (time) also confers suitability of the drugs. It was described that time is of two
kinds viz., First — such as Kshana, Lava, Muhurta etc. Second — stages of the patient (or
disease). Time (seasons) is of three characters — cold, hot and rainy. Between cold (Shishira)
and hot (Grishma) seasons and in between rainy (Varsha) and Cold (Hemanta) seasons,
there are two ordinary seasons (temperate) Viz., Vasanta and Sharat respectively. The
ordinary (temperate) season found in between Grishma and Varsha which is the first
commencement of rain is known as Pravrat.

The ideal time for administration of therapies like emesis and purgation is the day
time of the ordinary (temperate) seasons; whereas the daytime of other seasons are unsuit-
able because of the risk of inadequate or excessive bouts. Similarly, the ordinary seasons
are ideal even for the administration of other drugs (or treatments) to the body, because of
mild degrees of cold, heat and rain during these seasons and so are easy and harmless.
Opposite will be the condition in other seasons.

wad | ortker armrattagara | dguteatt waaetreasat seed Uradataada Ta
Teg: wRefarmgerieenrateraaaa  yHaaalufeaaaa-
AATagae WAT water (AF. FT 23/9)

----- PAGE 14 -----
6 Dravyaguna Vijnana

During cold season, the body being a victim of severe cold and cold breeze the
doshas become stable (immobile, stagnated) and heavy (greatly increased). Purificatory
drugs (or therapies) administered during this period, though generally of hot potency, get
vanquished by the profound cold, becomes inefficient in action, give only inadequate
bouts and make the body susceptible to many diseases of Vata origin.

Similarly, during rainy season also, similar is the result (the drugs become weak and
produce only inadequate bouts) because in this season the sky is full of thick clouds
which obstruct the hot rays of the sun reaching the earth, the earth becomes full of slush
by lashing rain and flowing water, the bodies of men get water logged, lose the efficiency
of digestive activity and become weak by the effect of season.

Waa | AVaATATATA TA aT wort Hata eiwet Hererary TT —
Aredtaee Saree tl (37.4. J 23/10)

The condition of the entire vegetation will be poor in potency because of the net-
work of roots of trees will have been submerged in water due to rain falling in torrents
from the clouds, the stem and branches will become full with tender leaves and buds,
making the trees appear to have become young. Because of this tenderness and the effect
of slush of the soil they will undergo sourness during digestion; the water will have be-
come contaminated with the cadavers, tissues, urine and excreta of birds, animals, reptiles
etc., the wind will be very cold due to presence of water vapour, snow and mist; the heat
of the earth becomes reduce, the vegetation not attaining maturity with potency, they will
only cause burning sensation during digestion in the human body. Because of these rea-
sons, the drugs which are collected even at the commencement (of the rainy season) will
be unsuitable for health (and produce inadequate bouts only) due to preponderance of
water and wind full of water vapour.

During Grishma (Summer) due to the effect of withdrawal (of moisture by the sun)

----- PAGE 15 -----
_Bheshajavacharaneeyam (Pre-requisites of Treatment) 7

the body under the influence of hot and dry wind will have severe perspiration and the
doshas will be very loose (fluid moving freely) and in very little quantity. Purificatory
drugs though not hot in potency will acquire the properties of heat and penctrating deep
inside, Cause excessive bouts (of emesis and purgation); the body will suffer from compli-
cations like thirst, giddiness and exhaustion,

So, itis ideal to administer emesis and other purificatory therapies during appropriate
seasons only, if there is no emergency discase. In case of an emergency conditions
purificatory treatment should be done by creating conditions opposite of the seasons arti-
ficially and administering drugs which have been made suitable with appropriate process-
ing, combination, dosage, mode of use and other procedures.

Bhaishajya Sevana Kala (Time of administration)

MKAAY GT Het: Ale Wat: | Taser arse AM
AeaTeAetet careteet attra: waft | PA HTSMNST AO, TTT | ST WATE
FETS UAHA TH ATS EHS: MM MATT PGT (a. 23/12)

In the context of the conditions or the stages of the patient (or the disease), time
means the different parts, viz., a certain time whether appropriate for administration of
medicine, or not, whether a particular time either suitable or unsuitable for a particular
drug etc. drugs administered either at premature time or elapsed time not yield the desired
results.

The times of administration of medicines are eleven in number such as without food,
before meals, after meals, mixed with food during its preparation itself, in between meals
(of afternoon and night), at the commencement and end of the meals, often (frequently)
irrespective of meals, mixed or along with morsel, in between such morsels and at night.

1. Abhakta
Tanith Amdetdee feterefedtei 1 ctxenfeepryreratar:
Wedoaqagata | gateq watfearareenter fect arferetrent safe 1
(A.4. Y 23/13)

Abhakta (without food) is administration of the drug separately without giving any
food to the patient. The action of the drug in that case will be very powerful. So, persons
who are strong, drugs should be given in the morning after the increased Kapha has
moved out of the stomach; in case of others it should be before food (of the morning or
breakfast) as the person will not suffer from fatigue by the consumption of food.

2. Pragbhakta
Rath AM Fea, | eM oe aay: HAT THATS AAT A earhtry,

----- PAGE 16 -----
Dravyaguna Vijnana

gerrare asftencorref er aren i (31.4. F 23/14)

Pragbhakia is administration of the drug just prior to the meal, It useful in the treat-
ment of diseases caused by Apana Varta, in diseases of lower extremities for strengthening
the body and for making the body thin.

3. Madhyabhakta
Teh Tet THT ae BAe frat | pteery a eareery thrhg a
(AA. FJ 23/15)
Madhyabhakta is administration of drug in the middle ofthe. meal_and useful in

diseases caused by Samana Vata, in diseases of alimentary tract and in diseases of Pittaja
origin.

4. Adhobhakta
SUNT WAHT | TT aaa UIA UA: Maa ys
mrret a aed aady etry a years wychenronedast Ni (3.8. | 23/16)

Adhobhakta is administration of drug after the meal, diseases due to Vyanavata and
should be after the morning meal and in diseases due to Udana vata it should be after the
evening meal. This method is useful for strengthening the upper part of the body and to
cure the diseases of Kapha and cause stoutness of the body.

5. Sabhakta
Rag ST ATG Ut (AA-Z,23/17)

Sabhakta is taking the food prepared by mixing the drug or mixing the drug to
prepared food and eating immediately. It is indicated for children, persons of tender con-
stitution, those who hate medicines, in loss of appetite and in diseases affecting the whole
body.

6. Antarabhakta

aT Ue Yate uh sitet were Aoaqagerd 1 afeever sfot qa
ae war Ubreateare | area areararay i (31.8. J 23/18)

Antarabhakta is administration of medicines first at midday after the digestion of the

forenoon meal (breakfast) and once again in the afternoon after the digestion of the mid-
day meal. Likewise in case of the night also. This method is indicated for those who have
strong digestion and in disorders caused by Vyanavata.

7, Samudga

HUTA MEAT TY ST VT 1 (31.4. FY 23/19)

Samudga is the use of the medicine twice- once just before meals and immediately
after meals. Food should be easily digestible and little in quantity, drugs should be having
digestive property and be in the form of confection, powder etc. this method is indicated
in hiccough, tremors, convulsions and when the doshas are localized at both upper and
lower parts of the body.

& Muhurmuhu

(AA. F 23/20)

Muhurmuhu is the use of medicine, frequently with or without food. This method is
useful in diseases like dyspnoea, cough, hiccough, thirst, vomiting and diseases caused
by poisons.

Bheshajavacharanecyam (Pre-requisites of Treatment) 1]

The doshas — Kapha Vata and Pitta - which have undergone increase in cold, hot
and rainy seasons respectively should be expelled out of the body either during the three
ordinary months — the Chaitra, Shravana, and Kartika or during the three ordinary Months
of Pravrat, Sharat and Vasanta Rutu respectively with the prescribed procedure. Having
created conditions opposite of the cold, hot and rainy seasons artificially treatments should
be done in appropriate time, without skipping the sequences of time.

If no relief is found within seven days another alternate treatment should be started
and that only after the effect of the earlier treatment has disappeared. Mixing of two kinds
of treatment is not good. Even it slight improvement has been obtained by any one treat-
ment, the same should be continued with suitable modifications to obtain better improve-
ment.

Quast yudeaewncaesst Yt: | Wert agyyt agaes: wether

satarisafed: aan aaa: | seated uftet: waraqwasra, i

(AA. FY 23/33-34)

The drug to be administered to the king, should be pleasant, small in quantity, with
no deleterious effects, pure, scientifically prepared, possessing many good qualities and
found efficacious many times. The physician should not engage himself in any other
work, should obtain the consent of his friends for his treatment; the drug having been
tasted by the royal routine earlier and also by himself and then administered to the king.

shat we at terest ete faery | aviszonit cadena |

VATA SISTA AT Ul (3.8. F 23/35-36)

That drug which is grown in the same land in which a person lives will be ideal for

that person. Those drugs which possess properties similar to the properties of the land in
which a person is born should be used for those living in a different land.

The drug, which by nature, is potent, fortified further by soaking it in the fresh juices
of others, made easily digestible, endowed with pleasant taste, smell etc. administered at
proper time, in the mild dose and with steady mind is sure to become nectar.

Research Corner

1. The plant has been found to possess hypoglycemic activity. (Pande M.C.,
Journal of natural integrated Med Ass, 1978, 20 (8), 295. The methanolic
extract of C. phlomidis Linn. (MECP) leaves shows most of the
pharmacological activities characteristic of minor tranquilizers. (Murugesan T.,
Saravanana K.S., Lakshmi S., Ramaya G. and Thenmozhi K., Evaluation of
psychopharmacological effects of Clerodendrum phlomidis Linn. Extract (J)
Phtomedicine, 2001, 8 (6), 472).

2. Methanolic extract of leaves of C. Phlomidis Linn (MECP) showed significant
inhibitory activity against castor oil induced diarrhoea and PGE2 induced
enteropooling in rats. The extract also showed a significant reduction in
gastrointestinal motility in charcoal meal test in rats. (Rani S., Ahamed N.,

3. The tre

a _ Agnimantha 15

Rajaram S, and Saluj

aR., Thenmozhi S., Murugesan T., J. Ethano-pharmacol.,
1999, 68, 315),

‘tment with petroleum ether, chloroforms and methanolic fractions of
alcoholic extract of Clerodendrum phlomidis at a dose of 500-mg/kg b.w,p.o.
significantly prevented CCl4 induced elevation of liver enzymes. The results
indicated that the methanolic fraction was most active among the three fractions
(Amita verma et al/Int.J.Pharm Tech Res. 2009, | (4) pp 1028-1031).

. Petroleum ether, Ethyl acetate and Methanolic extracts of Clerodendrum

phlomidis aerial parts at the doses of 100 and 200 mg/kg was evaluated for the

Analgesic activity using the hot plate and acetic acid induced abdominal

constrictions in mice. The study showed that the Methanolic and Ethyl acetate

extracts of Clerodendrum phlomidis at dose 200 mg/kg exhibited significant
analgesic activity. (R. Vijayamritharaj, S. Vincent and N. Senthilkumar.,

Analgesic activity of clerodendrum Phlomidis linn. (Aerial parts), International

Journal of Research in Pharmaceutical and Biomedical Sciences, Vol. 2 (1),

2011 pp 120-123),

. The methanolic extract of the roots of Clerodendrum phlomidis (Verbenaceae)
showed significant in-vitro anti-oxidation activity in the anti radical, superoxide
scavenging, anti lipid peroxidation, hydroxyl radical scavenging and nitric
Oxide scavenging, assays, (Gokani, R.H., et al, Evaluation of antioxidants
activity (in vitro) of Clerodendrum phlomidis, linn. f. suppl. Root, Journal of
Herbal Medicine and Toxicology 5 (1), 2011 pp 47-53).

- The anti inflammatory activity of the crude ethanolic extract of Clerodendrum
phlomidis was studied using the carrageenan and cotton pellet induced
inflammatory models. The antiarthritic activity was studied using Freund's’s
complete adjuvant induced rat model. Clerodendrum phlomidis displays
considerable potency in anti inflammatory action and has prominent antiarthritic
effect on adjuvant induced arthritis. (Babu NP, Pandukumar P, lgnacimuthu S.,
Lysosomal membrane stabilization and anti-inflammatory activity of
Clerodendrum phlomidis L.f., a traditional medicinal plant. J.
Ethnopharmacol. 2011. Jun 1;135(3):779-85. Equb 2011 Apr 16).

. The leaf extract of C. Phlomidis, when subjected to TLC, showed the presence

of adrenaline. Mohan Maruga Raja MK, Patel RS, Mishra SH, identification

and quantification of adrenaline from the leaves of Clerodendrum phlomidis
using thin-layer chromatography, Journal of Chinese Integrative Medicine,

9(1), 2011.

Morphology

Premna integrifolia Linn.

External Morphology : Premna integrefolia Linn.

A large shrub or a small tree reaching 9 mtrs in height.

Bark - Yellowish, lenticellate, Trunk and large branches
sometimes spinous.

Leaves - 5-9 cm long and 3-6 cm wide, broadly elliptic,
obtuse, very shortly acuminate, glabrous, entire or upper part
dentate, base rounded or sub acute, with 4-5 pairs of main
nerves, petioles 1 cm long.

Inflorescence - Terminal, Pubescent, Paniculate corym-
bose cyme.

Flower - Small greenish yellow, with disagreeable
odour.

Fruits - Pear shaped, small 4 mm long.

Clerodendron phlomides Linn

A large bush or small tree, reaching 9 mtrs high with
more or less pubescent branches.

Leaves - Ovate or subrhomboid, coarsely crenate, den-
tate or sub entire, glabrous above more or less puberulous
beneath. Base truncate, or subcordate, simple or opposite.

Inflorescence - Dichotomous axillary cyme, arranged to form a rounded terminal
panicle.
Flower - White or pinkish.
Fruit - Drupe, obovoid, black when ripe.

Karma (Actions)

Niryasa (Latex) : Grahi, Madaka, Ruchikara, Agnivardhaka, Stambhaka,

Phalatwak (Fruit bark) : Grahi, Mohakara, Ruchya, Agnivardhaka
Bija (Seed) : Balya, Vrashya

Pharmacological Actions

Analgesic, Hypnotic, Sedative, Narcotic, Respiratory depressant, and Depresses
cough reflex, Emetic (but in large doses suppresses vomiting centre).

Dosha karma (Action on Dosha) : Niryasa is Kaphahara because of its Ushna
virya. Bija is Pittahara because of its Shita virya.

Rogaghnata (Indications)
Niryasa (Latex): Shula (Pain), Atisara (Diarrhoea) and Agnimandya (Loss of appe-
tite)
Bija (Seed): Daurbalya (General debility), Klaibya (Impotency)

Phytoconstituents

Alkaloids of Opium are divided into two groups Viz., Phenantherene group and
Benzyl isoquindine group.

Phenanthrene séries - Benzyl isoquinoline group

Morphine 521%
Codeine 03.4%
Thebaine 03%

Opium also contains Meconin or pianin, organic acids like Neopine, Porphyroxine
and Meconidine.

Research Corner

1. High-performance liquid chromatography (HPLC) separations using a
monolithic column with acidic potassium permanganate and tris (2,2'-
bipyridyl)ruthenium (II) chemiluminescence detection in a rapid and highly
sensitive method to monitor the process of extracting opiate alkaloids from
Papaver somniferum. Due to the high flow rates allowed with the monolithic
column and the inherent selectivity of the chemiluminescence reactions, the
four predominant alkaloids-morphine, codeine, oripavine and thebaine-were
determined in less than 2 min. (Costin JW, Lewis SW, Purcell SD, Waddell
LR, Francis PS, Barnett NW. Rapid determination of Papaver somniferum
alkaloids in process streams using monolithic column high Acta. 2007 Jul
30;597(1):19-23. Epub 2007 Jun 27).

2. Solid-phase microextraction (SPME) with DVB/Carboxen/PDMS Stable-Flex
fiber was applied to the study of volatile compounds of several seed oil samples
from Papaver somniferum L. (Papaveraceae). 1-Pentanol (3.3-4-9%), 1-

----- PAGE 31 -----
Ahiphena 23

hexanal (10.9-30.9%), 1-hexanol (5.3-337%), 2-pentylfuran (7.2-10.0%), and
caproic acid (2.9-11.5%) could be identified as the main volatile compounds in
all examined poppy seed oil samples. (Krist S, Stuebiger G, Unterweger H,
Bandion F, Buchbauer G. Analysis of volatile compounds and triglycerides of
seed oils extracted from different poppy varieties (Papaver somniferum L.). J
Agric Food Chem. 2005 Oct 19;53(21):83 10-6).

3. Papaver somniferum allergy is mediated by an IgE mediated mechanism and
not by a pharmacological or toxic effect of the alkaloids or polyphenols.
(Moneo I, Alday E, Ramos C, Curiel G. Occupational asthma caused by
Papaver somniferum. Allergol Immunopathol (Madr). 1993 Jul-Aug; 21
(4):145-8).

4. Oripavine was isolated from the dried capsules of a variety of Papaver
somniferum cultivated in Tasmania. (Nielsen B, Roe J, Brochmann-Hanseen E.
Oripavine-a new opium alkaloid. Planta Med. 1983. Aug; 48(8):205-6).

5. The stenciling of alkaloid biosynthetic genes using VIGS confirms the
physiological function of enzymes previously characterized in vitro, provides
insight into the biochemical regulation of morphine biosynthesis, and
demonstrates the immense potential for metabolic engineering in opium poppy.
(Wijekoon CP, Facchini Pj. Systematic knockdown of morphine pathway
enzymes in opium poppy using virus-induced gene silencing. Plant J. 2012.
Mar; 69(6):1052-63. doi: 10.1111/j.1365-313X.2011.04855.x.Epub 2011 Dec

28.)

Reet - Tap root, branched.

Varieties

I. According to the Colour of flowers [Ra.Ni.

& Ni. Ratnakara)

1. Jarana — Digests food

4. Sarana — Evacuates wastes

1. Papaver glabrum Bios — Cultivated in Turkey, Egypt and Iran.

2 Papaver album DC — Cultivated in India & Yugoslavia
3. Papaver nigrum DC — Cultivated in Southern Europe
4. Papaver setigerum DC — Wild in Southern Europe
Different preparations
1. Raw opium (the inspissated juice): It is the dried latex obtained by the

incision from the unripe fruit capsules of Papaver somniferum, dried or partly
dried or partly dried by heat or spontaneous evaporation and worked into some
what irregularly shaped masses or moulded into masses of more uniform size
and shape.

2. Poppy seeds: Seeds are collected from the poppy capsules. The seeds are very
small, about 1 to 1.5 mm in diameter.

----- PAGE 34 -----
26 Dravyaguna Vijnana

3. Poppy straw: Dricd opium poppies are referred as ‘‘Poppy straw’’
Adverse effects and remedial measures
Signs and symptoms of acute poisoning
There are three stages:
1. Stage of Excitement: Sense of well being
(i) Freedom from anxiety
(ii) Restlessness
(iii) Hallucination
(iv) Flushing of face
(v) Increased action of heart
(vi) In children it may present with convulsions
2. Stage of Stupor: Depressive action starts
(i) Headache
(ii) Heaviness of the body
(iii) Fatigue
(iv) Giddiness
(v) Drowsiness and intense desire to sleep
(vi) Pupils contracted and pinpointed.
(vii) Face and lips slightly cyanosed
(viii) Pulse and respiration is normal
3. Stage of Coma:
(i) No response to talking and shaking.
(ii) Later, no response to deep pain.
(iii) Relaxed muscles, reflexes are lost.
(iv) All secretions except that of skin are depressed.
(v) Pupils contracted and insensitive to light.
(vi) Feeble pulse, slow.
(vii) Low blood pressure
Treatment
1. Gastric lavage with Potassium Permanganate (KMn04)
2. Endotracheal clearance

----- PAGE 35 -----
Ahiphena 27

3. Hot bags and blankets should be used to maintain the body heat.

4, Administering the antidote - Nalorphene hydro bromide in the dose of 5 mg IV
should be given.

Tinctureopium - 3to4ml

Habitat

Ajamoda grows all over India but commonly cultivated in Maharashtra state.

Morphology

Habit - An annual herb. Grows up to 1 mtr in
height

Leaves - Lower leaves are broad; where as upper
leaves are linear-lanceolate.

Inflorescence - Umbel inflorescence.

Flowers - White or greenish white and very small.

Fruit - Very small, Cremocarp 2 mm long.
Grahya- agrahya (Genuine characteristics):

Fruits of Ajamoda are ovoid or globular shaped,
yellow or grayish yellow in colour and up to 2 mm long.
The surface is studded with blunt simple hair. Fruit con-
sists of two mericarps, each having five ridges and fif-
teen vittae. Has got characteristic odour.

Research Corner

1. Adil MD, Kaiser P, Satti NK, Zargar AM, Vishwakarma RA, Tasdug SA,
Effect of Emblica officinalis (fruit) against UVB-induced photo-aging in
human skin fibroblasts. J Ethnopharmacol. 2010 Oct 28; 132 (1) : 109--14.
Equp 2010 Aug 3. Fruit of Emblica officinalis effectively inhibits UVB-
induced photo-aging in human skin fibroblast via its strong ROS scavenging
ability.

2. Gopa B, Bhatt J, Hemavathi KG, A comparative clinical study of
hypolipidemic efficacy of Amla (Emblica officinalis) with 3-hydroxy-
3methylgutaryl-coenzyme-A reductase inhibitor simvastatin. Indian J
Pharmacol. 2012 Mar; 44(2):238-42. Amla produced significant hypolipidemic
effect along with a reduction in blood pressure. Addition of Amla to the
currently available hypolipidemic therapy would offer significant protection
against atherosclerosis and coronary artery disease, with reduction in the dose
and adverse effects of the hypolipidemic agents.

3. Shivananjappa MM, Joshi MK, influence of Emblica officinalis aqueous
extract on growth and antioxidant defence system of human hepatoma cell line
(Hep G2]. Pharm Biol. 2012 Apr; 50(4):497-505. Epub 2011 Dec 1.

4. Dhingra D, Joshi P, Gupta A, Chhillar R, Possible Involvement of
Monoaminergic Neurotransmission in Antidepressant-like activity of Emblica
officinalis Fruits in Mice. CNS Neurosci Their. 2012 May; 18(5):419-25.

----- PAGE 44 -----
36 Dravyaguna Vijnana

doi:10.1111/).1755-5949.2011.00256.x. Epub 2011 Jun7. Thus, aqueous
extract of E. officinalis showed antidepressant-like activity probably by
inhibiting MAO-A and GABA; and also due to its antioxidant activity.

5. Patel SS, Goyal RK, Prevention of diabetes-induced myocardial dysfunction in
rats using the juice of the Emblica officinalis fruit. Exp Clin Cardiol. 2011 Fall;
16 (3):87-91.

6. Kumar A, Tantry BA, Rahiman S, Gupta U, Comparative study of
antimicrobial activity and phytochemical analysis of methanolic and aqueous
extracts of the fruit of Emblica officinalis against pathogenic bacteria J Tradit
Chin Med. 2011. Sep;31(3):246-50. Aqueous infusion extract of amla
exhibited potent antimicrobial activity against E. cloacae followed by E. coli.
Preliminary phytochemical analysis of E. officinalis aqueous extracts (infusions
and decoctions) only showed presence of tannins, saponins, flavonoids,
terpenoids and phenols. MIC of aqueous extract of E. officinalis was most
active against K. pneumoniae. Whereas minimum inhibitory concentration
(MIC) of methanol extract of E. officinalis shows maximum activity against E.
coli. Emblica officinalis definitely possesses potent antimicrobial.

Amalaki 39

Female flowers — Few, subsessile, ovary 3-celled.

Fruit — 1-1.5 cm diameter, fleshy, globose, with 6 ob-
scure vertical furrows, pale yellow of three 2-seeded crusta-
ceous cocci.

Seed — 6, 3-gonous.
Varieties
Raja Nighantu - Amalaka
Kshudamalaka
1. Vanya — Small & hard |
2. Gramya — Big, Soft & Fleshy Emblica afficinalis
Grahya Laxana (Genuine Charavteristics) Gach

Dried fruit is globular, subhexagonal, upto 1.5 cm in diameter, with a wrinkled sur-
face of yellowish brown or greyish black colour. The fruit breaks easily, exposing a
section of dried pulp and nut which contains triangular seeds of yellowish brown colour.

Fresh fruit is globular, fleshy, obscurely six lobed and measures 2 to 3 cm in diam-
eter. The surface is smooth and pale or yellowish green in colour. The fruits contain a
juicy pulp enclosing an obtusely obovate, Triangular, 3 celled nut, containing two seeds
ineach cell.

Achyranthes aspera Linn.

Grahya Laxana (Genuine Characteristics)

Dry herb, consisting of pieces of quadrangular, striated stems, 4 to 7 mm in diameter,
orbicular, obovate, hairy leaves, having an attenuated base and short petiole and pieces of
long slender spikes bearing prickly flowers or fruits which are sharply deflexed against
the rachis. The colour of fleshy dried material is greyish which changes to pale brown
during storage and will have faintly aromatic odour and bitter taste.

Research Corner

1. Cassia fistula stem bark was used for the preparation of aqueous extract and
synthesis of god nanoparticles to evaluate the hypoglycemic effects and found
that gold nanoparticles have promising antidiabetic properties. (Daisy P,
Saipriya K. Biochemical analysis of Cassia fistula aqueous extract and
phytochemically sythesized gold nanoparticles as hypoglycemic treatment for
diabetes mellitus. Int. J. Nanomedicine. 2012;7:1189-202. Epub 2012 Mar 7).

2. Antibacterial and antifungal potentials of hydroalcohol extracts of leaves of
Cassia fistula Linn found very effective. (Bhalodia NR, Shukla VJ.
Antibacterial and antifungal activities from leaf extracts of Cassia fistula L. :
An ethnomedicinal plant. J Adv Pharm Technol Res. 2011 Apr; 2 (2):104-9).

3. Bioactivity-guided fractionation of n-hexane phase from MeoH extract of the
seeds of Cassia fistula L. (Leguminosae) yielded two bioactive substances
against Cladosporium cladosporioides and C. sphaerospermum. (Sartorelli P,
Lago JH, Cunha RL, Kitamura RO, Young MC. A new minor dimmeric ester
from seeds of Cassia fistula L. (Leguminosae). Nat Prod Res. 2012;26(1):36-
41. doi:10.1080/1478619.2010.5321128. Epub 2011 Jul 15).

4. Methanolic extract of seeds of Cassia fistula did not produce any significant
toxic effect in mice. (Jothy SL, Zakaria Z, Chen Y, Lau YL, Latha LY,
Sasidharan S. Acute oral toxicity of methanolic seed extract of Cassia fistula
in mice. Molecules. 2011. Jun 23;16(6):5268-82).

5. Cassia fistula fruit pulp and seed extract possessed anticandidal activity.
(Irshad, Shreaz S$, Manzoor N, Khan LA, Rizvi MM. Anticandidal activity of
Cassia fistula and its effect on ergosterol biosynthesis. Pharm Biol. 2011 Jul;
49(7):727-33. Epub 2011 May 18).

6. Crude hydro-alcoholic extract of Cassia fistula (golden shower tree) fruit to
protect the kidney against bromobenzene-induced toxicity was confirmed by
the histological examination of the kidneys. (Kalantari H, Jalali M, Jalali A,
Salimi A, Alhalvachi, F, Varga B, Juhasz B, Jakab A, Kemeny-Beke A,
Gesztelyi R, Tosaki A, Zsuga J. Protective effect of Cassia fistula fruit extract
on bromobenzene-induced nephrotoxicity in mice. Hum Exp Toxicol. 2011
Oct; 30(10:1710-5. Epub 2011 Jan 19).

7. Hepatoprotective an antioxidant activities of ethanolic leaf extract (ELE) of

----- PAGE 61 -----
Aragwadha 53

Cassia fistula Linn. against diethylynitrosamine induced liver injury in ethanol
pretreated rats was found very effective. (Pradeep K, Raj Mohan CV,
Gobianand K, Karthikeyan S. Protective effect of Cassia fistula Linn. on
dicthylinitrosamine induced hepatocellular damage and oxidative stress in
ethanol pretreated rats. Biol Res. 2010;43(1):113-25. Epub 2010 May 7).

External Morphology

Habit — A medium sized perennial tree.

Stem — Erect, branched, cylindrical, woody, solid,
bark greenish grey in colour.

Leaf — Alternate, petiolate, compound, paripinnate,
with pulvinus base, leaf lets 4-8 pairs, stalked, ovate,
wavy, acute, glabrous, entire, venation of the leaflets | “
unicostate, reticulate.

Inflorescence — Long axillary pendulous racemes.

Flower — Pedicellate, with long pedicels, bracte-
ate, bisexual, complete, zygomorphic, yellow, hypogy-
nous and pentamerous.

Calyx-5 speals, slightly petaloid, polysepalous, odd ed
sepal anterior, imbricate aestivation. Cassia fistula Linn.

Corolla-5 petals, polypetalous, yellow, posterior a
petal is the small and innermost in the bud; petals unequally inserted on the thalamus.

Androecium-10 stamens, polyandrous and all with anthers, but very unequal in length.

Gynoecium-Monocarpellary, ovary superior, unilocular, with marginal placentation,
ovules many, short style, stigma is terminal and hairy.t

Fruit - A legume which is very long (1-2 feet) and cylindrical (nearly one inch in
diameter).

Seeds — Flat, albuminous, embedded in soft pulp.

----- PAGE 64 -----
56 Dravyaguna Vijnana

Grahya Laxana (Genuine Characteristics)

The fruit is a straight or slightly curved, cylindrical pod of chocolate brown colour,
with a short woody stalk and a blunt end which is suddenly contracted into a point. It
measures 40 to 70 cm in length and upto 2.5 cm in diameter. Internally the pod is divided into
anumber of cellular compartments by then woody transverse dissepiments. These compart-
ments contain flat, oval seeds of reddish brown colour and a soft black greasy pulp.

Commercially available material consists of seeds and walls of cells, having deposits
of black pulp. Pieces of the rind are also commonly mixed with the mixture.

Research Corner

1. Methanol, ethanol, acetone, aqueous (hot and cold) extracts from the leaves and
bark of Terminalia arjuna were tested for their antimicrobial activity and found
to be useful to treat the bacterial ear pathogens especially S. aureus. (Aneja KR,
Sharma C, Joshi R. Antimicrobial activity of Terminalia arjuna Wight & Arm.
: an ethnomedicinal plant against pathogens causing ear infection. Braz J.
Otorhinolaryngol. 2012 Feb; 78(1):68-74).

2. Methanol extract of Terminalia arjuna leaf (META) exhibited remarkable
antitumour activity against Ehrlich ascites carcionoma (EAC) in Swiss albino
mice (Biswas M, Bhattacharya S, Ghos AK, Kumar RB, Bera S, Gupta M,
Haldar PK, Antitumour activity of Terminalia arjuna leaf against Ehrlich
ascites carcinoma in mice. Nat Prod’Res. 2011 Oct 24. [Equb ahead of print).

3. Diethyl ether, ethyl acetate and ethanol fractions of Terminalia arjuna bark
exerted hypolipidemic and antioxidative effects at two different doses leves of
175 and 350 mg/kg body weight in. Poloxamer (PX)-407 induced

----- PAGE 68 -----
60

Dravyaguna Vijnana

hyperlipidemic albino Wistar rats. (Subramaniam S, Ramachandran S,
Uthrapathi S, Gnamanickam VR, Dubey GP, Antihyperlipidemic and
antioxidant potential of different fractions of Terminalia arjuna Roxb. bark
against PX-407 induced hfyperlipidemia, Indian J Exp Biol. 2011 Apr; 49
(4):282-8).f

. Aqueous extract of Terminalia arjuna [TA (AqE)]-induced cardiotonic action

via enhancing SR function, a unique action minimizing the occurrence of
arrhythmias, makes TA (AqE) a promising and relatively safe cardiotonic
beneficial to the healthy heart and the treatment for chronic heart disease.
(Oberoi L, Akiyama T, Lee KH, Liu SJ. The aqueous extract, not organic
extracts, of Terminalia arjuna bark exerts cardiotonic effect on adult ventricular
myocytes. Phytomedicine. 2011. Feb 15; 18(4):259-65).

. Methanolic Extract of Terminalia arjuna leaf demonstrated remarkable

antihyperglycemic activity in STZ (streptozotocin)-induced diabetic: rats.
(Biswas M, Kar B, Bhattacharya S, Kumar RB, Ghosh AK, Haldar PK.
Antithyperglycemic activity and antioxidant role of Terminalia arjuna leaf in
streptozotocin-induced diabetic rats. Pharm Biol. 2011. Apr;49(4):335-40.
Equb 2011 Feb 1).

. Terminalia arjuna bark powder (400 mg/kg, po) significantly reduced

formalin-indued paw oedema, showed anti-inflammatory potential against some
phlogistic agents along with some immunomodulatory activity and
antinococeptive action. (Halder S, Bharal N, Mediratta PK, fKaur I, Sharma
KK. Anti-inflammatory, immuno-modulatory and antinociceptive activity of
Terminalia arjuna Roxb bark powder in mice and rats.Indian J Exp Biol. 2009
Jul; 47(7):577-83).

. Arjunic acid, a strong antioxidant and a free radical scavenger, more potent

than ascorbic acid, in microsomes lipid peroxidation, DPPH, hydrogen
peroxide induced RBCs hemolysis, and (DCFH (2)-DA) assay (p<0.05). (Sun
FY, Chen XP, Wang JH, Qin HL, Yang SR, DU GH. Arjunic acid, a strong
free radical scavenger from Terminalia arjrruna. Am J Chin Med. 2008;
36(1):197-207).

Habitat

Arjuna is found as naturally growing plant in the dense forests through out India.
Arjuna can be successfully raised by sowing seeds.

External Morphology
A large tree with a huge buttressed trunk and horizontally spreading branches.

Bark — Smooth, Gray, flaking off in large flat pieces. Sap wood is reddish white and.
heart wood is brown.

Leaves — Sub-opposite, 10-15 cm long, 4-7 cm wide, oblong or elliptic oblong,

Arjuna 63

obtuse or subacute, pale dull green above, pale brown
beneath. Shallowly crenate, serrate in the upper part.
Base rounded or cordate, often unequal sided, main
nerves arcuate, 10-15 Pairs, reticulate venation. Peti-
ole is 0.5 to 1 cm long, with | or usually 2 prominent
glands at the top immediately below the leaves.f

Inflorescence - Short axillary spikes or termi-
nal panicles.

Flowers — Sessile, bracteoles linear, shorter than
flowers.

obovoid-oblong, fibrous, woody, glabrous, dark Terminalia arjuna Wight
brown with 5 hard projecting wings. and Avn

Grahya Laxana (Genuine Characteristics)

Flat or slightly curved, stratified pieces of stem bark or varying sizes.and 5 to 10 mm
thickness. External surface pink or flesh coloured with a mealy coating.

Inner surface is reddish brown finely striated, peeling out in thin flakes.

Ashoka 69

Malayalam

Habitat - Commonly grows in forest region of Western Peninsula, Central and
Eastern Himalaya and West Bengal. Plant grows very well near the streams or river banks.
It is cultivated in gardens throughout India for its handsome flowers.

Morphology — A tree grows 6-9 mtrs high,
branches are glabrous.

Leaves — 15-20 cm long, compound, rachis gla-
brous, corky at the base, petioles very short, stipules
are intrapetiolar, completely united.

Leaflets — 4-6 pairs, 10-20 cm long and 4-7 cm
wide, oblong-lanceolate, obtuse or acute, glabrous,
base rounded or cuneate, slightly oblique, petiolules
4-6 mm long, stout, wrinkled.

Inflorescence — Axillary corymb.

Flowers— Numerous, fragrant, 7.5-10 cm across, u
peduncles stout, pedicels 8-13 mm long, red, glabrous, Saraca indica Linn.
bracts ovate, subacute, bracteoles 2, appearing like a
calyx, 4 mm long, calyx passing from yellow to orange and finally red; tube 1.3-2 cm
long, cylindrical, solid at the base; segments 4, oblong or obovate-oblong, 1 cm long.
Stamens 7 or 8 ovary is pubescent.

Fruit — Pod, black, 10-25 cm long, 4.5-5 cm wide, linear oblong, tapering to both
ends, compressed, and glabrous.

Seeds — 4-8, ellipsoid-oblong, 3-8 cm long, slightly compressed.
Grahya Laxana (Genuine Characteristics)

Bark: Curved, channelled or sometimes quilled pieces, 5 to 12 mm in thickness.
Outer surface is rough, greyish brown in colour, covered with warty protuberances and
enclosing circular to transversely elongated lenticels. The inner surface of the bark is
smooth reddish brown in colour and finely mottled with black dots. Transversely cut
surface shows long, radially elongated but irregularly running medullary rays. It is tough
to break and becomes fibrous. Bark doesn't possess any characteristic odour.

1. Neha Sehgal, Alok Gupta, et.al., Withania somnifera reverses Alzheimer’s
disease pathology by enhancing low-density lipoprotein receptor-related protein
in liver, proceedings of the National Academy of Sciences of United States of

A 30-d course or oral administration of a semipurified extract of the root of

----- PAGE 82 -----

bo

Dravyaguna Vijnana

Withania somnifera consisting predominantly of withanolides and withanosides
reversed behavioral deficits, plaque pathology, accumulation of g-amyloid
peptides (Ab) and oligomers in the brains of middle-aged and old APP/PS1
Alzheimer's disease transgenic mice. It was similarly effective in reversing
behavioral deficits and plaque load in APPSwlnd mice (line j20). The
remarkable therapeutic cffect of W. Somnifera mediated through up-regulation
of liver LRP indicates that targeting the periphery offers a unique mechanism
of Ab clearance and reverse the behavioral deficits and pathology seen in
Alzheimer's disease models.

. Kaurav BP, Wanjari MM, Chandekar A, Chauhan NS, Upmanyu N, Influence

of Withania somnifera on obsessive compulsive disorder in mice, Asian Pac J
Trop Med. 2012 May; 5(5):380-4.

W. Somnifera extract was found effective in treating obsessive compulsive
disorder. Administration of aqueous extracts W. Somnifera (AEWS) and
methanolic extracts MEWS (50 mg/kg) successively decreassed the marble
burying behaviour activity without affecting motor activity.

. Hosny Mansour H, Farouk Hafez H. Protective effect of Withania somnifera

against radiation-induced hepatotoxicty in rats. Ecotoxicol Environ Saf. 2012
Jun; 80:14-9. Epub 2012 Feb 28.

WS could be developed as a potential preventive drug for ionizing irradiation
induced hepatotoxicity disorder via enhancing the antioxidant activity and
induction of HO-1.

Konar A, Shah N, Singh R, Saxena N, Kaul SC, Wadhwa R, Thakur MK.
Protective role of Ashwagandha leaf extract and its component withanone on
scopolamine-induced changes in the brain and brain-derived cells. Plos One.
2011;6(11):e27265. Epub 2011 Nov11.

Our study suggested that besides cholinergic blockade, scopolamine-induced
memory loss may be associated with oxidative stress and alcoholic extract of
Ashwagandha leaf, and withanone may serve as potential preventive and
therapeutic agents for ne-urodegenerative disorders.

Shah J, Goyal R., Investigation of neuropsychopharma-cological effects of a
polyherbal formulation on the learning and memory process in rats. J Young
Pharm. 2011 Apr;3(2):119-24.

The poly herbal formulation containing Ashwagandha produced significant
improvement in passive avoidance acquisition and memory retrieval in rats.

Ashwagandha 77

Habitat — Found throughout the dry parts of In-
dia, in waste places, on bunds in areas of upper ge-
netic plain, West Bengal, Bihar, Orissa, Gujarat,
Konkan, Deccan and Karnataka. It grows in semitropi-
cal areas receiving 500-750 mm rain fall. Crop requires
relatively dry season during its growing period.
External Morphology

Habit — A much branched wild peremial herb.

Root -— Tap-root branched.

Stem — Acrial, erect, cylindrical, branched, her-
baceous above, woody below, densely covered with
fine stellate hairs.

Leaf -—Cauline and ramal, alternate or in unequal
pairs, ovate, ovate-oblong, or ovate lanceolate, entire,
acute or obtuse, tapering to the base; petiolate, petiole | Withania somnifera(L) Dunal

thick and long; reticulate unicostate ventation, covered
with glandular branched hair.

Inflorescence — Sub-sessile, axillary umbellate, 5- flowered cyme.

Flower — Greenish yellow, subsessile, pedicellates, complete, bisexual, pentamer-
ous, actinomorphic and hypogynous.

Calyx —5 Sepals, gamosepalous, campanulate (bell shaped), tomentose.

Corolla —5-Petals, short, campanulate, greenish yellow in colour.

Androecium — Stamens 5, free

Gynoecium — Bicarpellary, syncarpous, superior, bilocular and axile placentation.

Fruit — Small berry, globose, yellow or red when ripe.

Seed — Endospermic.

Grahya Laxana (Genuine Characteristics)

Root—Cylindrical, straight or slightly tortuous, mostly unbranched roots, 5 to 8 mm
in diameter, occurring whole or in pieces of varying length; upper portion of the roots are
more often topped with stem bases. Outer surface fawn coloured or light brown, finely
wrinkled longitudinally : a transversely cut portion of the root shows a creamish starchy
surface with a thin, closely adhering bark, followed by a narrow cortex and a large woody
portion cortex and a large woody portion surrounded by a prominent cambium ring of
brownish colour.

4. In the absence of a desired first choice medicinal herb, classical Ayurveda

Ayurvedic texts and lexicons give specific examples of possible substitutes.

88 Dravyaguna Vijnana

Regional Names

Indian Atees
Habitat

Ativisha is a drug of Himalayan origin. It is commonly found in the sub alpine and
alpine zones of the Himalaya from Indus to Kumaon at a height ranging about 6000-
15000 ft.

Morphology

Habit — Herbaceous perennial plant grows 1 to 1.5
mrs.

Root — Both taproot and adventitious roots are
present. Adventitious roots are swollen to form tubers.

Stem — Aerial as well as underground, aerial stem is
erect, hairy & branched.

Leaves — Cauline and ramal, simple, petiolate, leaf
base sheathing.

Inflorescence — Raceme with an end flower.

Flowers — Bisexual, polyse-palous, polypetalous,
with numerous stamens, bi to pentacarpellary syncarpous,
superior ovary.

Fruit —Etaerio of follicles

Varieties :
Based on colour of root tubers: esdanidae oa yom
Madanapala Nighantu
1. Rakta — Red tubers
2.Shweta — White tubers
3. Krishna -— Black tubers
4. Pita — Yellow tubers

----- PAGE 97 -----
Ativisha 89

Botanically three varieties of Afivisha are observed:

1, Aconitum heterophyllum

2. Aconitum palmatum

3. Aconitum kashmiricum
Grahya Laxana (Genuine Characteristics)

Tuberous roots are obconical or ovoid, sometimes occurring as a pair of mother and
daughter tubers. The daughter tuber is bigger in size being 2 to 6 cm Jong and upto 1 cm
broad in the middle. It ends in a thin tap like extremity which is sometimes divided. The
surface is smooth, slightly curved and has a yellowish grey colour. The mother tuber is
small, shriveled up to 3 cm long and 0.5 cm thick and has a dark grey colour. The roots
are often crowned with scaly leaf buds.

Adverse effects

Ativisha belongs to the genus of poisonous species, shows the effect of poisoning
when used without proper care. At extreme over doses shows mild to moderate poisoning
like constipation, dry mouth, brady cardia but not fatal. 5-6 gm in a single dose without
purification may produce these toxicological effects.

Antidote — Goghrata (Cow’s ghee) or Godugdha (Cow’s milk) orally.
Shodhana (Purification)
Ativisha tuberous roots are cut into small pieces and kept in Gomaya kwatha (Liquid
cow dung) for 3 hours and then dried in Sunlight.

Habitat

Grows in Uttar Pradesh and Assam, fairly common in Bihar, Punjab, Rajasthan,
Deccan, Konkan, and Karnataka, Commonly grows in plain areas and it is cultivated
also.

Morphology

Habit — An erect annual herb grows 60-180 cm
in height. Trunk is straight and gland dolted. Branches
are strong and elastic.

Leaves — Alternate, simple, covered with numer-
ous black dots. 3 to 8 cm long and 2.5 to 5 cm broad.
Round or heart shaped, scallop toothed with mucr-
onate apex. 5 main nerves springing from the base
and 4-6 pairs of lateral nerves springing higher up of
the leaf. Petiole is 1-2.5 cm long.

Inflorescence — Axillary 10-30 flowered race-
mose.

Flowers — Bluish purple in colour, long peduncle
and short pedicels. Calyx 3-4 mm long, gamosepalos.
Corolla twice as long as calyx, standard petal is or-
bicular 6 mm long, clawed, wing petal is oblong and
keel petal is obtuse.

Psoralea corylifolia Linn.

Androecium contains 10 stamens.

Gynocecium has monocarpellary ovary with long style and a minute terminal stigma.

Fruit — A small blackish, glabrous, ovoid or kidney shaped, compresed, closely
pitted one seeded indehiscent legume. Pods are 5 mm long.

Seeds — Smooth, small, adhering to pericap, some what ovate with an agreeable
aroma.

----- PAGE 104 -----
96 Dravyaguna Vijnana

Grahya Laxana (Genuine Characteristics)

Fruit is one seeded, indehiscent pod, measuring up to 5 mm in length and 3.5 mm in
breadth. These are ovoid, oblong, somewhat compressed or flattened. The surface of the
pericarp which closely adheres to the seed is glabrous, closely pitted, having a dark chocolate
to almost black pitted, having a dark chocolate to almost black colour. The entire pod, in
most cases, is found to be enclosed within a membranous persistent calyx which is adher-
ent to the fruit. The seed is also black and contains two starchless cotyledons. Odfrour is
faint, which turns pungent on chewing, having acrid, bitter taste and is nauseating.
Adverse effects

Oral administration has resulted in nausea, vomiting, malaise, loose motion and head
ache. Oil has a specific irritant action on the skin and mucous membrane.

On applying on leucoderma, the skin become reddish and occasionally blisters may
occur.

Mac

Habitat —Bala is distributed throughout the hotter parts of India and fairly common
in various provinces in states like Uttarpradesh, West
Bengal, Karnataka, Andhrapradesh, Tamilnadu and
Kerala. It is acommon weed grows in waste places.

Morphology
Habit — Wild undershrub

Stem — Herbaceous, aerial, erect, cylindrical,
branched, solid and green.

Leaf - Cauline and ramal, alternate, stipulate,
cordate, serrate, acute, pubescent, unciostate reticu-
late reined.

Inflorescence — Solitary, axillary or terminal and
few flowered cymes.
Flower —- Bracteate, pedicellate, complete,

actionomorphic, bisexual, pentamerous, hypogynous,
yellow coloured, 5 sepals, gamosepalous, petals are Sida cordifolia Linn

----- PAGE 111 -----
Bala 103

5, polypetalous, twisted, yellow. Stamens are indefinite, monadelphous; gynoecium is
multicarpellary, syncarpous, multi locular due to false septa.

Fruit —Schizocarp., 6-8 mm in diameter.

Grahya Laxana (Genuine Characteristics):

Identification: Pieces of cylindrical, gradually tapering roots upto 2 cm in diameter.
branching at lower parts only. The outer surface is rough, longitudinally wrinkled or
shrivelled and earthy brown in colour. The thicker root pieces bear transversely elongated
lenticels. Thinner pieces representing lower part of root bear lot of root-scars and rem-
nants of rootlets. A transversely cut portion of the root whose closely adhering bark which
is up to 1 mm thick; a well marked cambium ring, a woody region characterised by
radiating medullary rays in between concentric ring and a central pith. On breaking be-
comes short woody fieces, has characteristic pungent odour, mucilaginous on chewing.

Adverse effects

Bala is known as Indian-ephedra because it contains 0.8-1.2% of Ephedrine. The
continuous use of products with Ephedrine which is an alkaloid can bear a series of risk to
health. Intake of Bala, by not being aware of presence of ephedrine, or by ignoring its
contra-indications, or a prolonged use, specially combined with other stimulants, can be
harmful. For the reasons many states of United States have prohibited the use of the
ephedrine in the dietary compliments and not to advise in home made preparations. If
used it may cause Hypertension, Headache, Tremors, Insomnia, and Tacchycardia etc.

Remedial Measures: Avoid a prolonged usage of products containing ‘Ephedrine’

methanolic extract of Pferocarpus marsupium Roxb and Ocimum sanctum
Linn reverses dyslipidemia and oxidative stress in alloxan induced type I
diabetic rat model. Exp Toxicol Pathol. 2012 Jul; 64(5):441-8. Epub 2010 Nov
23.

4, The present study was aimed at extensively evaluating the antioxidant
properties of an anti-diabetic plant extract of stem bark of Pterocarpus
marsupium using various in vitro radical scavenging assays as well as by using
liver slice cultures as a model system. Results demonstrate that the whole
aqueous extract showed high antioxidant activity in all different assays used
and also protected mitochondria against oxidative damage. Mohammadi M,
Khole S, Devasagayam TP, Ghaskadbi SS. Pterocarpus marsupium extract
reveals strong in vitro antioxidant activity. Drug Discover Ther. 2009 Aug;
3(4):151-61.

5. The present study was undertaken to evaluate cardiotonic activity of aqueous
extract of heartwood of Pterocarpus marsupium. Compared to digoxin, a drug
with narrow therapeutic window, Pterocarpus marsupium showed wide
therapeutic window. Mohire NC, Salunkhe VR, Bhise SB, Yadav AV.
Cardiotonic activity of aqueous extract of heartwood of Pterocarpus
marsupium. Indian J Exp Biol. 2007 Jun; 45(6):532-7.

Grahya Laxana (Genuine Characteristics)

Heart wood pieces will be rectangular or square shaped, 2 to 3 cm thick. Surface is
yellowish red with golden hue, marked with dark streaks and parallely running striation
which represent annual rings. The drug also occurs as curved shavings of brownish red
colour. Very difficult to break and it is brittle. Has phenolic characteristic odour. Taste is

astringent followed by bitter after taste.

Bhallataka 117

Morphology

Bhallataka is a medium sized tree grows up to
30-50 ft. in height.

Bark — Gray in colour and exudes an irritant se-
cretions on incising.

Leaves-— Simple, 30-60 cm long, 12-30 cm broad,
obovate to oblong, glabrous above, ashy gray or buff
and pubescent beneath and developed on the tip of the |£7,”
branch and deciduous. ;

Inflorescence — Compound panicle.

Flowers-— Small, greenish yellow, dioecious, with
long flower stalk, petals are 5-6, stamens 5-6, ovary is
rudimentary in male flowers and superior in case of | Semecarpus anacardium Linn.
female flowers.

Fruit— Drupaceous nut, 2-3 cm long, heart shaped, green when raw and turn to
black on ripening. Fruits are attached to fleshy orange coloured receptacle, which is ed-
ible.

Seeds - Pendulous.

Fruit contains black, oily and highly vesicant juice which is used traditionally for
marking the cloths.

Varieties — Sushruta and Nighantu Ratnakara:

1.Bhallataka 2. Nadi Bhallataka

Botanically other species are:

1. Semecarpus albecens 2. Semecarpus travancoria
3. Semecarpus anrienlata 4. Semecarpus heterophylla
5. Semecarpus kurzi 6. Semecarpus pubescens
7. Semecarpus obscura 8. Semecarpus parviflora

Grahya Laxana (Genuine Characteristics):

Fruit is heart shaped, obliquely ovoid, drupaceous nut, with a fleshy pear-shaped
receptacle. It is up to 2.5 cm long and 2 cm broad. Surface is black or brownish black,
glossy, sometimes shrivelled. Internally the nut contains a viscous oily liquid of black
colour.

Adverse effects
In higher doses Bhallataka will cause

1. If consumed then gastric lavage should be done.
. In case of dehydration-I V fluid administration.
. Antacid therapy
. Jiraka (Cumin seed) and Saindhava lavana (Rock salt) to be given.

nn & WwW bh

. Coriander leaves paste is applied.
6. Butter with Tila kalka (Paste of Sesamum) or Haridra (Turmeric) application.

Antidote
1. Coconut oil will be good antidote.

2. Application of Meghanada Rasa (Juice of Amaranthus tricolour) with-
Navanita (Butter).

Fatal Dose — More than 10 gm.
Fatal Period — 12-24 hrs.

Shodhana (Purification)

1. Fruit of Bhallataka (Semecarpus anacardium) is cut into pieces by crushing
and kept it in Ishtika churna (Brick powder) or rubbed with brick powder.
Then it is washed clearly with hot water. This purifies Bhallataka.

2. Swedana is done with Narikela ambu (Coconut water) is Dolayantra.

Research corner

1. A new compound, serratin, was isolated from the essential oil of Clerodendron

serratum alongwith lupeol. The structure of the compound has been elucidated
on the basis of chemical analysis and spectroscopic methods including IR, MS,
and NMR techniques. Ravikumar R, Lakshmanan AJ, Ravi S. Chemical
constituents from Clerodendron serratum. J Asian Nat Prod Res. 2008 Jul-
Aug; 10(7-8):659-62.

2. The ethanol extract of Clerodendron serratum roots and ursolic acid isolated
from it were evaluated for hepatoprotective activity against carbon tetrachloride
induced toxicity in male Wistar strain rats. The results when compared with the
standard drug silymarin, revealed that the hepatoprotective activity of the
constituents ursolic acid is significant as similar to the standard drug. Vidya
SM, Krishna V, Manunatha BK, Mankani KL, Ahmed M, Singh SD.
Evaluation of hepatoprotective activity of Clerodendrum serratum L. Indian J
Exp Biol. 2007 Jun; 45(6):538-42.

. The alcoholic extract (50, 100 and 200 mg/kg, p.o.) of Clerodendron serratum
roots produced a significant antinociceptive, anti-inflammatory and antipyretic
activities in animal models. Narayanan N, Thirugnana-sambantham P,
Viswanathan S, Vijayasekaran, V, Sukumar E, Antinociceptive, anti-
inflammatory and antipyretic effects of ethanol extract of Clerodendron

Regional Names

Habitat— Bharangi grows in many parts of India like Karnataka, Kerala, Tamilnadu,
West Bengal, Bihar and Himalayan region.

Morphology

Habit — Shrub grows upto 7 ft. in height.

Stem — Bluntly quadrangular, young parts gla-
brous.

Leaves— Opposite, big, upto 28 cm long but usu-
ally 12-15 cm in length, 5.5-6 cm wide, oblong or
elliptic, acute, coarsely and sharply serrate, glabrous,
petioles are very stout, 6 mm long.

Inflorescence— Panicle, 15-20 cm long.

Flowers— Numerous, showy has a pair of acute
bracts at each branching, pedicels twisted, flowers
are bluish to dark purple in colour.

; Clerodendron serratum (Linn).
Fruit — Drupe, 6 mm long, somewhat succulent, ile Cinp)

broadly obovoid, normally four lobed.

Grahya Laxana (Genuine Characteristics)

Roots are hard, cylindrical, gradually tapering, 2 to 5 cm in diameter, split longitudi-
nally into half. External surface is rough, dark brown in colour, with scattered patches of
small beady warts and circular scars. The centrally split pieces show a creamish yellow,

----- PAGE 133 -----
Bharangi 125

longitudinally striated woody surface with a well marked pith. The bark is upto 2 mm
thick and is always accompanied with substantial portion of woody part of the root. Root
is difficult to break and becomes fibrous. Root does not have any particular odour.

Adulterants
1. Stem part of Clerodendron serratuin
2. Root of Clerodendrum indicum
3. Bark of Picrasma quasiodes

Research corner

1 The anti-diabetic effect of the ethanolic extract of Eclipta alba (EEA) on
hyperglycaemia and diabetic nephropathy was investigated in streptozotocin-
induced diabetic rats. The results suggest that EEA possesses antidiabetic effect
associated with alpha-glucosidase and aldose reductase inhibition. Jaiswal N,
Bhatia V, Srivastava SP, Srivastava AK, Tamrakar AK, Antidiabetic effect of
Eclipta alba associated with the inhibition of alpha-glucoside and
aldosereductase. Nat Prod Res. 2012 Feb 21.

2 Study was designed to explore antiproliferative activity of Eclipta alba on liver
and other related cancer. The data establish the existence of anti-proliferative,
DNA damaging and anti-metastasis properties in EAE which is yet unexplored
and hold high therapeutic impact. Chaudhary H, Dhuna V, Singh J, Kamboj

----- PAGE 137 -----
Bhringaraja 129

SS, Seshadri S. Evaluation of hydro-alcoholic extract of Eclipta alba for its
anticancer potential: an in vitro study. J. Ethnopharmacol. 2011 Jun
22;136(2):363-7-6. Epub 2011 May 6.

3. The present study investigated the ability of 100 and 200mg/kg of aqueous
extract of Eclipia alba to circumvent aggression. Eclipta alba significantly
minimized dominance (p<0.05) which is correlated to the level of aggression
particularly with 200 mg/kg in the water competition test. A tangible behavioral
submission was observed with 100 and 200mg/kg and of Eclipta alba in the
foot shock induced test. Lobo OJ, Banji D, Annamalai AR, Manavalan R.
Evaluation of anti aggressive activity of Eclipta alba in experimental animals.
Pak J. Pharm Sci. 2008 Apr; 21(2):195-9.

4. The combined effect of dried Eclipta alba leaf powder (3 g/day) in
encapsulated form on blood pressure, diuresis, and lipid profile of 60 mildly
hypertension male subjects in the age group of 40-55 years was studied. In
conclusion, it would appear that E. alba is diuretic, hypotensive, and
hypocholesteroliemic and helps in the alleviating oxidative stress-induced
complications in hypertensions. Rangineni V, Sharada D, Saxena D. Diuretic,
hypotensive and hypocholesterolemic effects of Eclipta alba in mild
hypertensions subjects: a pilot study. J Med Food. 2007 Mar; 10(1):143-8.

5. The anti-inflammatory effect of the plant of Eclipa alba (Family-Asteraceae)
was evaluated using carrageenin, mediators such as histamine and serotonin
induced paw oedema, and cotton pellet induced granuloma tests for their effect
on acute and chronic phase inflammation models in rats. The results indicate
the potent anti-inflammatory effect and therapeutic efficacy of Eclipta alba
extract on animal models, which is compared with Indomethacin. Kumar SS,
Sivakumar T, Chandrasekar MJ, Suresh B. Evaluation of Anti-inflammatory
Activity of Eclipta alba in rats. Anc Sci Life. 2005 Jan;24(3):112-8.

Bhringaraja 131

Habitat

Bhringaraja is found throughout India ascending
to 6000 ft altitude from sea level especially nearby
Marshy places as a weed. Commonly seen in South
Indian states like Karnataka, Kerala, Andhra Pradesh,
Tamilnadu and grows abundantly in Uttar Pradesh,
Gujarat, Maharashtra, West Bengal ete.f

Morphology
Habit — Annual herb.
Root- Branched, tap root

Stem- Herbaceous, aerial, erect, cylindrical,
branched, solid, glabrous, hairy.

Leaf —Ramal and cauline, simple, opposite, decussate, sub-sessile, exstipulate, el-
liptical, acute, reticulate venation.

Inflorenscence — Capitulum: ray florests (periph-
eral flowers) are ligulate, and disc florets (Central flow-
ers) are tubular; involucre of bracts present.ftt

Ray floret — Bracteate, sessile, incomplete, zy-
gomorphic, unisexual, pistillate, tetramerous, epigy-
nous, cyclic, and peripheral in position. Sepals are 4,
white, petals are 4. Androecium is absent or highly
reduced. Ovary is inferior. Gynoecium is bicarpellary
syncarpous, Disc florets are sessile, complete, actino-
morphic, bisexual, tetramerous, white, central in po-
sition, sepals are 4, reduced to pappus Petals are 4,
gamopetalous, tubular stamens are 4, Gynoecium is
bicarpellary syncarpous, inferior ovary and unilocu-
lar.

Grahya Laxana (Genuine Characteristics)

Dried whole herb with flowers and fruits; stems, cylindrical up to 4 mm thick, red-
dish brown in colour; leaves, long lanceolate, narrowed at both ends, having a rough
surface of dark green colour which is covered with white apressed hair; flower heads are
in pairs, axillary or terminal, with white ray florets and yellow or black disc (when ripe).
It has mild odour and bitter taste.

Research corner

A study done to assess hepatoprotective and antioxidant activities of 11 phyllanthus
species like P. amarus, P. urinaria, P. debilis, P. tenellus, P. virgatus, P. maderaspatensis,
P. reticulates, P. polyphyllus, P. emblica, P. indofischer and P. acidus. The methanol
extracts of P. polyphyllus, P. emblica and P. indofischeri showed high levels of
hepactoprective activity. Srirama R et all. “Hepatoprotective activity of Indian Phyllanthus.
Pharm Biol. 2012. Aug 50 (8):948-53.

Research corner

1 To evaluate ethanolic extract of leaves of Aegle marmelos in an experimental
animal model of chronic fatigue syndrome for potential therapeutic benefit. The
results are suggestive of potential protective effect of A. marmelos against
experimentally induced CFS. Lalremruta V, Prasanna GS. Evaluation of
protective effect of Aegle marmelous Corr. In an animal model of chronic
fatigue syndrome. Indian J. Pharmacol. 2012 May; 44(3):351-6.

2 Antioxidative role of Aegle marmelos in aspirin-induced gastroduodenal
ulceration in albino rat was evaluated using essential biochemical parameters.
Pretreatment with Aegle marmelous fruit pulp extract for 14 consecutive days
showed the reverse effects of aspirin suggesting gastro-duodenal protective and
anti-ulcerogenic properties of Aegle marmelos through its antioxidants
mechanism. Das SK, Roy C. The protective role of Aegle marmelos on aspirin-
induced gastro-duodenal ulceration in albino rat model: a possible involvement
of antioxidants. Saudi J. Gastroenterol. 2012 May-Jun; 18(3):188-94.

3 To evaluate antiproliferative and anxtioxidant activity of Aegle marmelos
leaves in Dalton’s Lymphoma Ascites (DLA)-bearing mice. The

5 Study done to evaluate the immunomodulatory potential of methanol extract of
Aegle marmelos in an experimental animal model of cellular and humoral
immunity. Low dose of methanol extract of Aegle marmelos was more

inclined towards humoral immunity. Govinda HV, Asdaq SM.

Grahya Laxana (Genuine Characteristics)

Root Curved or channeled pieces of root bark of varying sizes, 3-5 mm in thick-
ness. Outer surface is grayish white or carthy brown in colour, prominently warty in the
case of older roots. The younger root shows few warts but a large number of lenticels
which appear as short vertical slits, Surface skin is fairly tough and layered, but feels off in
small irregular pieces on rupturing. Inner surface is fibrous, having greenish brown colour.

Research corner

1 The study is conducted to evaluate the neuroprotective potential of Bacopa
monnieri (BM), and Indian traditional medicinal plant effective against
cognitive impairment, in colchicine-induced Dementia. The results suggest
therapeutic potential of Bacopa monnieri in the treatment of AD associated
cognitive decline. Saini N, Singh D, Sandhir R. Neuroprotective Effects of
Bacopa monnieri in Experimental Model of Dementia. Neurochem Res. 2012
Sep; 37(9):1928-37. Epub 2012 Jun 15.

----- PAGE 157 -----
Brahmi 149

2 Study done to test the hypothesis that BM Extract (BME) could offset
neurotoxicity induced oxidative dysfunctions in developing brain in a rotenone
(ROT) mouse model. It is suggested that Bacopa monnieri may be effectively
exploited as a prophylactic/therapeutic adjuvant for neurodegenerative
disorders involving oxidative stress. Shinomol GK, Mythri RB, Srinivas
Bharath MM, Muralidhara. Bacopa monnieri extract offsets rotenone-induced
cytotoxicity in dopaminergic cells and oxidative impairments in mic brain, Cell
Mol Nurobiol/ 2012 Apr; 32(3):455-65. doi:10.1007/s10571-011-9776-0.
Epub 2011 Dec 10.

3 An n-butanol extract of the plant (nBt-ext BM) was analysed and found to
contain Bacoside A (Bacoside A3, Bacopaside II and Bacopasaponin C), The
effects of the BM extract were then studied on morphine-induced hyperactivity
as well as dopamine and serotonin turnover in the stratum since these
parameters have a role in opioid sensitivity and dependence. These findings
suggest that nBT-ext BM has an antidopaminergic/serotonergic effect and may
have potential beneficial effects in the treatment of morphine dependence. Rauf
K, Subhan F, Sewell RD. A Bacoside containing Bacopa monnieri extract
reduces both morphine hyperactivity plus the elevated striatal dopamine and
serotonin turnover. Phytother Res. 2012 May; 26(5):758-63. doi:10.1002/
ptr.3631. Epub 2011 Nov 22.

4 Study is done on Anthihyperglycemic activity, in vivo antioxidant potential,
effect on hemoglobin glyco-sylation, estimation of liver glycogen content, and
in vitro peripheral glucose utilization of bacosine, a triterpene isolated from
ethyl acetate fraction (EAF) of the ethylenic extract of Bacopa monnieri. Study
suggested that bacosine might have insulin-like activity and_ its
antihyperglycemic effect might be due to an increase in peripheral glucose
consumption as well as protection against oxidative damage in alloxanized
diabetes. Ghosh T, Maity TK, Singh J. Antihyperglycemic activity of bacosine,
a triterpene from Bacopa monnieri, in alloxan-induced diabetic rats. Planta
Med. 2011 May; 77(8):804-8. Epub 2010.Dec 10.

5 In this study, the neuroprotective properties of Bacopa monnieri against
rotenone induced oxidative damage and neurotoxicity is studied. Flies (Oregon
K strain, adult males) exposed to a standardized Bacopa monnieri powder for
7 days in the diet exhibited significant diminution in the levels of endogenous
oxidative markers viz., malondialdehyde, hydroperoxide and protein carbonyl
content. Hosamani R, Muralidhara. Neuroprotective efficacy of Bacopa
monnieri against rotenone induced oxidative stress and neurotoxicity in

----- PAGE 158 -----
Drosophila melanogaster. Neurotoxicology. 2009 Nov; 30(6):977-85, Epub

2009 Sep 8.

Regional Names

Habitat

Brahmi is found through out India growing naturally in the planes as well as in hills
up to 1200 mtr attitude particularly in the areas having more moisture. It is very common
in Karnataka, Maharashtra, Orissa, Uttarpradesh, Bihar etc.

Morphology

Habit — Succulent, spreading, prostrate or
creeping glabrous annual herb. Branches are nu-
merous, upright and having adventitious roots at
almost every node. Stem is green with internodes
of varying length.

Leaves — Simple, opposite, decussate, almost
sessile, spathulate or obovate-oblong shape, entire
and fleshy. Leaves are obscurely veined and dot-
ted with minute spots 0.5-2 cm long and 0.5 cm
wide.

Flowers— Axillary, solitary on long slender
pedicels of varying lengths usually longer than the
leaves. Flowers are whitish or pale bluish in colour.
Petals are twice as long as the sepals. Stamens are 4. Ovary is bicarpellary syncarpous,
two chambered with many ovules in each cell on swollen placenta.

Fruits — Capsule, ovoid, small, 2-celled, 2-valved and glabrous.
Seeds— Oblong, minute and many.

152 Dravyaguna Vijnana

Grahya Laxana (Genuine Characteristics)

Fresh herbs consists of quadangular, succlent, creeping stems, upto 8 mm thick,
bearing fleshy leaves occuring in whorls at nodes; the leaves are decussate, spathulate,
oblong, obovate and upto 2.5 cm in length; the stems bear clusters of white thin wiry roots
on the underside; flowers are solitary, axillary and have a pink perianth.

The dried material is yellowish in colour with majority of leaves detached. It has
mild odour and bitter in taste.

Controversy

Number of plants has been described in Ayurveda as Medhya (Brain tonic). Brahmi
and Mandukaparni are two popular names in this category. The controversy on Brahmi
and Manukaparni is never ending.

In Ayurvedic classical texts like Charaka samhita, Sushruta Samhita and Ashtanga
Hradaya, Brahmi and Mandukaparni have been described as two different plants. For
examples Caraka enumerated Aindri and Mandukaparni together in the Prajasthapana
varga which may be considered as Bacopa Monnieri and Centella asiatica respectively.
The controversy may be because of synonyms and similar properties.

But these two plants may be distinguished easily.

Research corner

1 A new characteristic steroidal glycoside of the 23S, 26R-hydroxylated
spirostanol-type named indioside F was isolated from the fruit of Solanum
indicum, along with indioside A and protodioscin. El-Aasr M, Miyashita H,
Ikeda T, Lee JH, Yoshimitsu H, Nohara T, Murakami K. A new spirostanol
glycoside from fruits of Solanum indicum L. Chem Pharm Bull (Tokyo). 2009
Jul; 57 (7):747-8.

2 Solavetivone (1), Cytotoxic to OVCAR-3 cells with an IC (50) value of 0.1
mM, has been isolated from Solanum indicum. Syu WJ, Don MJ, Lee GH, Sun
CM. Cytotoxic and novel compounds from Solanum indicum. J Nat Prod.
2001 Sep; 64(9):1232-3.

----- PAGE 164 -----
156 Dravyaguna Vijnana

3. Beta-Sitosterol (S]-0), beta-sitosterol glucoside (S]-1), dioscin (SI-2), methyl
protoprosapogenin A of dioscin (SI-3), methyl protyodioscin (SI-4) and
protodioscin (SI-5) were isolated and characterized from the whole plant of
Solanum indicum L. (Solanaceae) showed cytotoxicity on seven cancer cel]
lines. Chiang HC, Tseng TH, Wang CJ, Chen CF, Kan WS. Experimental
antitumor agents from Solanum indicum L. Anticancer Res. 1991 Sep-Oct;
11(5):1911-7.

4. The ethanol extract of roots, fruits and roots of Solanum indicum and
Saccharum munja respectively and water soluble resin of commiphora myrrha
were studied for anti-inflationary activity were found in former two plants will
slight anti inflammatory activity was observed in latter plant. Singh RK, Joshi
VK, Gambhir SS. Anti-inflammatory activity of some traditional medicinal
plants. Anc Sci Life. 1998 Oct; 18(2):160-4.

5. A standardized extract of the fruits of Solanum distichum has previously been
shown to possess anti-hypertension activity in rats (ED50 about 1 mg/kg).
Earlier acute toxicity studies had shown that single doses up to 2g/kg orally or
intraperitoneally failed to show any signs of morbidity. To study its potential
long-term toxicity, rats of either sex were fed rurally 1g/kg of the extract daily
over a period of 4 weeks to 6 months the present findings point to the wide
safety margin of the extract, Abdel Aziz UW, Fawzy N, Ismail Al, El-Askary H.
Toxicological studies on a standardized extract of Solanum indicum ssp.
distichum. Food Chem Toxicol. 2011 Apr; 49(4):903-9. Epub 2010 Dec 22.

Habitat

Brahati is found throughout tropical India,
grows in waste places, along roadsides and in the
places where it gets good sunshine.

Morphology

Habit — Armed shrub

Stem — Herbaceous, branched, green, prickly;
prickles scattered.

Leaves — Simple, appear opposite (from adna-
tion); those of the same node unequal, sinuate,
sparsely stellate-pubescent above; densely so below;
prickles absent on leaves; exstipulate.

Solanum indicum Linn.

Inflorescence —Extra-axillary corymbose cyme.
Flower — Ebracteates, bisexual, ebracteolate, obliquely, zygomorphic, hypogynous.
Calyx — Cupular, lobes pubescent, lobes enlarged in fruit.
Corolla: Petals Stellate— pubescent, acute, white, sub-rotate, plicate.
Androecium — Stamens 5.
Gynoecium — Superior ovary, bilocular.
Fruit- Berry, green, globose, pulpy.
Seeds — Circular, smooth.
Varieties
Ra.Ni- 1. Brahati 2. Sarpatanu
3. Shwetabrahati
Grahya Laxana (Genuine characteristics)

Pieces of stout, tortuous roots upto 4 cm in diameter, sometimes divaricately branched,
with long cylindrical rootlets. External surface is rough, earthy brown in colour, with
small round warty protuberances. Root bark separates easily from the root, exposing a
longitudinally striated surface of yellowish white colour. A transversely cut portion shows
a thin bark, followed by a cortical layer enclosing a broad mealy woody portion and a

----- PAGE 167 -----
Brahati 159

a a nent ret tin ne a —

central pith surrounded by a prominent ring. The dried root is difficult to break and be-
comes short pieces. It has slightly pungent odour and bitter to taste.

Different species
1, Solanum indicum 2. Solanum torvum
Substitutes
1. Solanum torvum Swartz
2. Solanum incanum Linn.

Prapunnata (Cassia tora) seeds are pounded with sour substances and then applied

Research corner

A study done to investigate the effects of soluble fibres of Cassia tora seeds on lipid
metabolism. Male sprague dawly rats were fed one of three experimental diets, a normal
diet a high cholesterol diet or a high cholesterol diet with 5% soluble fibres of Cassia tora
seeds for 5 weeks. Serum concentration of total cholesterol in rats fed soluble fibres of
Cassia tora was 27% lower compared to that of the control group. The results indicate
that it enchances fecal lipid excretion and may cause a reduction in serum and hepatic
lipid concentration in rats. Cho IJ, Lee C & Ha T%. “Hypolipidemic effect of soluble
fiber isolated from seeds of Cassia tora Linn. in rats fed a high cholesterol diet.” J. Agric
food Chem 2007 Feb 21:55(4):1592-6.
