Regional Names
Kannada Baje

Malayalam Vayambhu

Habitat
Vacha is culitvated throughout India. It is also found wild in Himalayan region and
Sikkim.
Morphology
Habit — A perennial terrestrial herb of marshy
places.

Stem — Stout, creeping
Rhizome — Is aromatic.

Leaves — Two-ranked, distichous, sword shaped
(ensiform), erect, sharp pointed, closely sheathing each
other, midrib is stouts, striate parallel venation, and
leaves are glabrous, weakly differentiated into petiole
and blade.

Inflorescence —Spadix, scapes 3-angled, keeled
on the back, spathe is leaf-like long, green, narrow.

Acorus calamus Linn (Vaca)

Flower — Flower densely cover the entire spadix, bisexual, trimerous, compactly
arranged, actinomorphic, hypogynous, greenish yellow, bracts and bracteoles absent.
Perianth contains six tepals in two equal series, narrowly oblong, equalling the pistil with
vaulted tips. Androecium contains six stamens in two whorls of three each. Gynoecium is
tricarpellary, syncarpous; ovary is trilocular with few ovules.

Fruit —Turbinate, prismatic and top pyramidal.

Varieties
Ra.Ni- 2types: 1. Vacha - Acorus calamus, 2. Shweta vacha — Iris germanica

----- PAGE 688 -----
O80 Dravyaguna Vijnana

Grahya Laxana (Genuine characteristics)

The Rhizome is sub-cylindrical or compressed, upto 20 cm long and 1 to 1.5 crn
broad, having a light brown colour. The upper surface shows triangular leaf scars, while
lower surface bear small, raised circular root scars. The fracture is short, granular and
porous. The cut pieces are some what circular, mealy white in colour and upto 1 cm thick.
Has got strong and characteristic odour, taste is pungent.

Research corner

1. The aim of this study was to investigate the antidiabetic activity of Crataeva
nurvala stem bark (Family: Capparidaceae) extracts in alloxan-induced diabetic
albino rats. C. nurvala pertroleum ether extract (CNPEE) and ethanolic extract
(CNEE) showed significant (P<0.001) anti diabetic activities. Sikarwar MS,
Patil MB. Antidiabetic activityof Crataeva nurvala stem bark extracts in
alloxan-induced diabetic rats. J. Pharm Bioallied Sci. 2010 Jan; 2(1):18-21.
doi: 10.4103/0975-7406.62700.

2. Chemical investigation of stem bark of Crataeva nurvala afforded 5,7-
dimethoxy-3-phenyl-1-ethyl-1, 4-dihydro-4-quinolone and a steroidal glycoside
with unprecedented pentacyclic ring system named crateamine (la) and
crataenoside (2) respectively. Sinha S, Mishra P, Amin H, Rah B, Nayak D,
Goswami A,Kumar N. Vishwakarma R, Ghosal S. A new cytotoxic quinolone
alkaloid and a pentacyclic steroidal glycoside from the stem bark of Crataeva
nurvala: study of anti-proliferative and apoptosis inducing property. Eur J. Med
Chem. 2013 Feb; 60:490-6. dol: 10.1016/j. ejmech. 2012.12.017. Epub 2012
Dec 17.

3. In this study, lupeol, a pentacyclic triterpene isolated from Crataeva nurvala
stem bark, and its ester, lupeol linoleate, were investigated for their possible
hypocholesterolaemic effect against cyclophosphamide-induced lipidaemic
instabilities. Treatment with lupeol linoleate was more effective than leupeol in
rendering protection to the cardiac tissue challenged by cyclophosphamide.

Grahya Laxana (Genuine Characteristics)

Bark is channelled, flat or slightly curved pieces, outer surface is rough and scaly,
greyish black in colour, minutely fissured, bearing numerous small lenticels. Inner surface
is lemon yellow in colour, longitudinally striated. Transversely cut portion of bark shows
a porous surface with numerous dark yellow specks, representing vascular bundles. On
breaking it becomes short pieces, does not have any odour. Taste is slightly bitter.

Research corner

1. The effectiveness of aqueous extracts of various medicinal plants in
detoxification of aflatoxin B1 (AFB1) was tested in vitro by thin-layer
chromatography and enzyme-linked immunosorbent assay (ELISA). Among
the different plant extracts, the leaf extract of Vasaka (Adhatoda vasica Nees)
showed the maximum degradation of AFB1 (798%) after incubation for 24h at
370C. Vijayanandraj S, Brinda R, Kannan K, Adhithya R, Vinothini S, Senthil
K, Chinta RR, Paranidharan V, Velazhahan R. Detoxification of aflatoxin B1
by an aqueous extract from leaves of Adhatoda vasica Nees. Microbial Res.
2013 Aug 5. pii: $0944-5013(1300118-3. doi:10.1016/j.micres. 2013.07.008.
2. The purpose of this study was to characterize the most bioactive

phytochemicals viz., vasicine, vasicinone, vasicine acetate, 2-acetyl
benzylamine, vasicinolone present in the chloroform fraction having anti-
inflammatory and antimicrobial activities. The anti-inflammatory activity was
tested by using carrageenan and CFA-model induced paw oedema. The
observed results revealed that vasicine showed most potent anti-inflammaory
effects (59.51%) at the dose of 20.0 mg/kg at 6h after carrageenan inejection
and maximum inhibition rate was observed of vasicinone (63.94%) at the dose

of 10.0 mg/kg at 4 days after CFA injection. Singh B, Sharma RA. Anti-
inflammatory and antimicrobial properties of pyrroloquinazoline alkaloids from
Adhatoda vasica Nees. Phytomedicine. 2013 Mar 15;20(5):441-5. doi:
10.1016/j.phymed. 2012.12.015. Epub 2013 Jan 26.

3. The larvicidal activities of methanolic fractions from Adhatoda vasica leaf

extracts were investigated against the bancroftian filariasis vector Culex
quinquefasciatus and dengue vector Aedes aegypti. The results indicated that
the mortality rates was high at 100, 150, 200 and 250 ppm of methanol extract
of fractions III with R (f) value 0.67 and methanol extract of fraction V with
R (f) value 0.64 of A. Vasica against all the larval instars of C.
quinquefasciatus and A. aegypti. Thaingaivel A, Chandrasekaran R, Revathi
K, Nisha S, Sathish-Narayanan S, Kirubakaran SA, Senthil-Nathan S.

. Study demonstrated that both the compounds, vasicine acetate and 2-acetyl

tuberculosis. Ignacimuthu S$, Shanmugam N. Antimycobacterial activity of two
natural alkaloids, vasicine acetate and 2-acetyle benzylamine, isolated from
Indian shrub Adhatoda vasica Ness. leaves. J Biosci. 2010 Dec; 35(4):565-70.

. The present investigation was carried out to study the anti-ulcer activity of
Adhatoda vasica leaves using two ulcer models (1) Ethanol-induced, and (2)
Pylorus ligation plus aspirin-induced models. Results of the study suggest that
in addition to its classically established pharmacological activities, the plant also
has immense potential as an anti-ulcer agent of great therapeutic relevance.

. Shrivastava N, Srivastava A, Banerjee A, Nivasarkar M. Anti-ulcer activity of
Adhatoda vasica Nees. J Herb Pharmacother. 2006; 6(2):43-9.

Research corner

1. Ayurvedic method of detoxification of such products involves Shodhana.
Hence, in present paper it has been decided to replace Ayurvedic Shodhana
process by chemical purification method and to study the benefits and/or
drawbacks of the traditional Ayurvedic Shodhana process. Crude aconite root,
Ayurvedic Shodhana treated aconite root and chemical Shodhana treated
aconite root samples were evaluated for toxicity and changes by animal studies
and thin layer chromatography (TLC) respectively. The results of the toxicity
study suggest that the modified method of Shodhana is less efficient as
compared to the traditional Ayurvedic Shodhana process. TLC studies have
shown that pseudoaconitine and aconitine were converted into far less toxic
substances like vertroyl pseudoaconine and benzoylaconine respectively only
in traditional Ayurvedic Shodhana. Deore SL, Moon KV, Khadabadi SS,
Deokate UA, Baviskar BA. Evaluation of toxicity of ‘Vatsanabha’ (Aconitum
feerox, Ranuculaceae) Before and After Shodhana. J Young Pharm. 2013 Mar;
5(1):3-6. doi: 10.1016/j.jyp. 2013.01.001. Epub 2013 Mar 7.

2. Vatsanabha (Aconitum ferox Wall.) is a very well-known ingredient of
Ayurvedic formulations and is prescribed as an antipyretic, analgesic,
antirheumatic, appetizer and digestive. The recommended dose of purified
Vatsanabha (A. ferox Wall.) root is 15 mg. A case of hypotension and
bradycardia due to aconite poisoning caused by overdosing of an Ayurvedic
medicine (Mahashankaha Vati), which was primarily managed by Ayurvedic
treatment. Panda AK, Debnath SK. Overdose effect of aconite containing
Ayurvedic Medicine (‘Mahashankha Vati’). Int J Ayurveda Res. 2010 jul;
1(3):183-6. doi. 10.4103/0974-7788.72493.

3. Simple and rapid stability-indicating HPLC method were developed for the
individual analysis of aconitine (ACN) and piperine (PIN) in Mahamrutynjaya
rasa, and herbal dosage form containing Aconitum ferox, Piper nigrum, and
Piper longum in combination. Separation of the ACN from its major and minor
degradation products was successfully achieved on a reverse-phase C18

----- PAGE 705 -----
Vatsanabha 697

column (250x4.6 mm id, 5 microm particle size), with isocratic elution using
a mixture of acetonitrile-KH2PO4 buffer (10 mM, pH 8+/-0.1; 50+50, v/v) at
flow rate of 0.7 mL/min with UV detection at 227 nm. PIN separation was
performed on a reversed-phase C18 column (250x4.6 mm id, 5 microm particle
size), with isocratic elution in acetonitrile-KH2P04 buffer (10mM, pH 7+/-
0.1;35+65, v/v) at a flow rate of 1 ml/min with UV detention at 343 nm, Rai
P, Pathak A, Rajput SJ. Stability-indicating reverse-phase liquid
chromatographic methods for the determination of aconitine and piperine in a
polyherbal formulation. J AOAC Int. 2009 Jul-Aug; 92(4):1044-54,

----- PAGE 706 -----
698 Dravyaguna Vijnana

Botanical Name — Aconitum ferox Wall.
Aconitum — Aconite-Monk’s hood
ferox—Ferocious, Terrible, Very poisonous
Family — Rananculaceae (Refer Appendix-II for key features)
Kula - Vatsanabha kula
Ganavargikarana (Classical categorization)
Charaka  Notmentioned in Varga

Sushruta Not mentioned in Gana

Bh.Pr.Ni_ — Dhatwadi varga 192
Habitat

Vatsanabha is found in alpine and subalpine western Himalayan region at the height
of 6,000 to 10,000 ft. altitude.
Morphology

Habit — Herb

Roots — Biennial, paired, tuberous, daughter tube
is ovoid-oblong, to ellipsoid, 2.5 to 4 cm long, 1 to
1.5 cm thick, with few filiform root-fibres, dark brown
externally, mother tuber much shrunk and wrinkled
with more numerous root fibres.

Stem — Erect, 40 to 90 cm high, slender, cov-

ered with short spreading yellow hairs in the upper
part, glabrous below.

Leaves — Scattered, distant, petioles slender, 25 (Vatsanabha)
cm in lower leaves, upper leaves have short petioles.
Blade is orbicular-cordate to reform in outline with a rather wide sinus upto 8 cm deep, 11
cm high from the sinus to the tip, upto 20 cm across.

Inflorescence — Raceme, 10 to 25 cm long, rachis slender.

Flower — Slender pedicels, erect, sepals are blue, hairy, uppermost helmet shaped,
shortly beaked, lip deflexed, lanceolate, acute and entire. Carpels are five in number.

----- PAGE 707 -----
Vatsanabha 699

Fruit — Follicle, 1.5 to 2 cm long, 4 to 5 mm broad, almost glabrous.
Seeds — Obovoid to of pyramidal 2.5 to 3 mm long, winged along the rhaphe.

Visha laxana (Toxic signs and Symptoms)

As Vatsanabha (Aconitum ferox) is a ferocious poison it causes several harmful
effects. In a few minutes after consuming any of the preparations of aconite, there will be
tingling sensation followed by numbness in the lips, mouth, tongue and pharynx, fol-
lowed by salivation, pain in the stomach and vomiting. Tingling and numbness extend
over the whole body. The person sweats profusely and there will be a sensation of im-
pending paralysis. The person feels giddiness, his vision and speech will be impaired,
limb becomes weak, unable to stand or walk. Twiching of muscles and convulsions may
occur. Pulse will be slow and become irregular. First breathing will be rapid but soon
becomes slow. Pupils alternately contract and dilate but remain fully dilated in the later
stages. Death ensures from ventricular fibrillation or respiratory paralysis.

Fatal dose — 1 gm (Root), 250 mg (Extract) 4 mg (Alkaloid)

Fatal period — 20 minutes to 24 hrs. Average is 6 hrs.

Treatment
e Patient should be kept warm.
e Gastric lavage with tannic acid or Postassium permanganate solution.
¢ Heart and respiration support with hypodermic injections.
e Artificial respiration and oxygen supply if necessary.
Purification
1. Mature tubers of Vatsanabha (Aconitum ferox) are cut into small pieces (To
size of Bengal gram) and put into a vessel made of mud or stone filled with
Gomutra (Cow’s urine). This vessel is kept under hot sun. Fresh Gomutra is
used every morning and process is repeated for 3 days. Later tubers are
removed, deskined and dried.

2. Vatsanabha (Aconitum ferox) is cut into small pieces and placed in a Potali
formed out of cloth. This Potali containing Vatsanabha is immersed in a vessel

containing milk and boiled for 3 to 6 hours.
Antidote — Tankana (Borax)

Market sample

Market sample consist abconical, abruptly tapering roots, more often paired as mother
and daughter tubers, 2 to 5 cm in length and 2.5 cm in diameter at the thickest part.
Surface dark brown in colour, transversely shrivelled, and densely covered with remanants
of rootlets or root scars arranged in an annular fashion. On breaking become short pieces,
exposing a mealy surface of yellowish brown in colour. Doesn’t possess any odour, taste
is slightly sweet followed by tingling sensation.

Habitat
Vibhitaki is very common tree found in Indian
forests and plains.

Morphology
Habit — Large deciduous tree grows up to 60 ft.
height.

Leaves — Gathered at the extremities of the

branches, simple, alternate, coriaceous, 10-20 cm long, Terminalia et Roxb

----- PAGE 715 -----
Vibhitaki 707

7-15 cm wide, broadly elliptic or elliptic-obovate, rounded or rarely subacute, or shortly
acuminate, both surfaces are puberculous when young, margin is entire, base is narrowed,
main nerves are 6-8 pairs, spreading, prominent, midrib is prominent on both sides, peti-
ole is 2-10 cm long, without glands at the apex.

Inflorencence — Axillary slender spike.

Flowers— Greenish yellow, with an offensive odour, flowers in upper part of spikes
are male, which is shortly pedicelled and those in the lower part are hermaphrodite and
sessile.

Fruit—Drupe, 1 to 2.5 cm in diameter, ovoid, grey, suddenly narrowed into a short
stalk, velvety, obscurely S-angled when dried.

Grahya Laxana (Genuine characteristics);

Fruit is hard, ovoid or obovoid, more often pentagonal drupe, suddenly narrowed
into a short stalk or a scar of pedicel, up to 2.5 cm long and 2 cm broad. Surface is velvety,
covered with a close fulvous tomentum, having light amber colour. Fruits are dark brown
to black in colour and globular in shape. The fruit contains a hard stony seed with five
longitudinal ridges. The kernel is oily, having three prominent ridges. Fruit does not have
any characteristic odour.

Research corner

1. Antifungal activity of Embelia ribes was evaluated on eight different fungal
species by employing various concentrations of seed extract (0.5-2.0 mg). All
the concentrations of seed extract inhibited the fungal growth, whereas
maximum activity was observed at 2.0 mg concentration of seed extract. The
present study clearly demonstrated the antifungal properties of Embelia ribes.
Rani AS, Saritha K, Nagamani V, Sulakshana G. In vitro Evaluation of
Antifungal Activity of the Seed Extract of Embelia ribes. Indian J Pharm Sci.
2011 Mar;73(2):247-9.

2. The purpose of this study was to investigate the preventive effect of embelin
against hyperlipidemia and oxidative stress in HFD-induced obesity in rats. The
present study indicated the preventive effect of embelin in HFD-induced
obesity and its related complications. Embelin could be valuable in the
development of new drug therapies to prevent obesity, hyperlipidemia, and
oxidative stress. Chaudhari HS, Bhandari U, Khanna G. Preventive effect of
embelin from Embelia ribes on lipid metabolism and oxidative stress in high-

----- PAGE 719 -----
Vidanga 71
fat diet-induced obesity in rats. Planta Med, 2012 May;78(7):651-7. doi:
10.1055/s-0031-1298379. Epub 2012 Mar. 26

3. The present study was designed to investigate the protective effects of embelin
from Eribes on global ischemia/reperfusion-induced brain injury in rats.
Observations suggested that embelin is a neuroprotective agent and may prove
to be useful adjunct in the treatment of stroke. Thippeswamy BS, Nagakannan
P, Shivaharan BD, Mahendran S, Veerapur VP, Badami S. Protective effect of
embelin from Embelia ribes Burm. against transient global ischemia-induced
brain damage in rats. Neurotox Res. 2011 Nov; 20 (4):379-86. doi: 10. 1007/
s12640-011-9258-7. Epub 2011 Jul 13.

4. Anticonvulsant activity of embelin (2.5, 5 and 10mg/kg, i.p.) was studied. It
showed a significant inhibition of the seizures induced by electroshock and
pentylenetetrazole in a dose dependent manner and the activity was comparable
to phenytioin and diazepam. Significant decrease in locomotion revealing its
CNS depressant activity was observed. The findings suggest that embelin
possess anticonvulsant activity against both grand mal and petit mal epilepsy.
Mahendran S, Thippeswamy BS, Veerapur VP, Badami S. Anticonvulsant
activity of embelin isolated from Embelia ribes. Phytomedicine. 2011 Jan
15;18(2-3): 186-8. doi: 1016/j. phymed.2010.04.002. Epub 2010 Jun 1.

5. Forty days of orally feeding the aqueous E. ribes extract (100 and 200 mg/kg)
to streptozotocin (40 mg/kg, iv, single dose) induced diabetic rats produced
significant decrease in heart rate, systolic blood pressure, blood glucose, blood
glycosylated hemoglobin, serum lactate dehydrogenase, creatine kinase and
increase in blood glutathione levels as compared to pathogenic diabetic rats.
Bhandari U, Ansari MN. Antihyperglycaemic activity of aqueous extract of
Embelia ribes Burm in streptozotocin induced diabetic rats. Indian J Exp Biol.
2008 Aug; 46(8):607-13.

Habitat
Vidanga is found in hilly parts of India.

Morphology
Habit — Large scandent shrub.

Branches — Long, slender, flexible, terete with
long internodes.

Bark — Studded with lenticules

Leaves —5 to 9 cm long, 2 to 4 cm wide, ellip-
tic or elliptic lanceolate, coriaceous, shortly and ob-
tusely acuminate, entire, glabrous on both the sides,
shining above, paler and silvery beneath, whole sur-
face covered with scattered minute reddish sunken
gland. Base is rounded or acute, main nerves are
numerous. Petiole 0.5 to 1.5 cm long.

Inflorescence — Laxed terminal or axillary
panicled raceme. Branches are 7.5 to 10 cm long.

Embelia ribes Burn. F

Flowers— Numerous, small, greenish yellow
colored. Pedicels are 1.5 to 2 mm long. Glandular pubescent bracts are minute. Sepals
1.25 mm long, ciliate, petals are 5 in number, free, 4 mm long, stamens are 5, shorter than
the petals.

714 Dravyaguna Vijnana

Fruit — Globose, 3 to 4 mm in diameter, smooth, succulent, black when ripe, like a
pepper corn when dried, tipped with the persistent style.

Substitute

Grahya Laxana (Genuine characteristics): Market sample contains globular fruits
dull red or brown colour, up to 4 mm in diameter, more often with a five partite persistent
calyx and a short stalk. The surface of the fruit is warty. The thin pericarp encloses a
single seed of red colour which is enveloped in a delicate membrane. It has got faint spicy
odour, tastes pungent.

Rasadi panchaka

Pharmacological Actions

Hepatoprotective, Antimicrobial, Antiviral, Spasmolytic, Antiulcer, Antimutagenic,
Antipyretic, Antioxidant, Antiinflammatory and Expectorant.
Doshakarma (Action on Dosha)

Vata pitta shamaka. Vatahara due to Madhura vipaka and Madhura Rasa, Pitta
Shamaka due to Shita Virya, Mdhura Vipaka and Madhura rasa.
Rogaghnata (Therapeutic Indications)

Yashtimadhu - Dourbalya, Klaibya, Khalitya, Palitya, Shwaasa, Kasa, Swarabheda,
Vishavikara, Aruchi, Trishna, Daha, Vaivarnya and Sirashula.

Klitanaka - Trishna, Aruchi, Dourbalya, Klaibya, Raktapitta, Netra Vikara.

Phytoconstituents

Root contains glycyrrhizine, prenylated biaurone, licoagrone, liqcomarin, isoflavone,
glyzaglabrin, quercitin, kaempferol, stragaion, liquiritigenin, isoliquiritigenis, licuraside,
liquiritoside, liquoric acid, glabrotide, deoxoglabrolide, glycyrrhizic acid, liconicone,
blabridin, liqurazid, liquiritin, glabranine and glabrene

Research corner

1. Isoliquiritigenin (ISL), a chalcone and liquiritigenin (LTG), a flavonoid found
in licorice roots. ISL displays antioxidant, anti-inflammatory, antitumor and
heatoprotective activities whereas LTG is an estrogenic compound, acts as an
agonist selective for the b-subtype of the oestrogen receptor. Both the phenolics
were isolated from the rhizomes of Glycyrrhiza glabra. Compunds 1, 4 and 10
were selected for in vivo antidiabetic ‘activity and found to be potential
candidates for treatment of diabetes. Gaur R, Yadav KS, Verma RK, Yadav
NP, Bhkuni RS. In vivo anti-diabetic activity of derivatives of isoliquiritigenin
and liquiritigenin. Phytomedicine. 2013 Nov. 18. pii : S0944-7113 (13)00424-
8.doi : 10.1016/j. phymed. 2013.10.015.

2. The present study was designed to investigate the beneficial effects of Gg root
extract on learning and memory in 1-month-old male Wistar albino rats. Four

----- PAGE 726 -----
18 Dravyaguna Vijnana

doses (75, 150, 225, and 300 mg/kg) of aqueous extract of root of Gg was
administered orally for six successive weeks. Results showed that all the doses
of aqueous root extract of Gg significantly enhanced the memory; however, in
the doses of 150 and 225 mg/kg, it showed a significant (p <0.01) enhancement
in learning and memory. Ghakravarthi KK, Avadhani R. Beneficial effect of
aqueous root extract of Glycyrrhiza glabra on learning and memory using
different behavioral models; An experimental study. J. Nat Sci Biol Med. 2013
Jul;4(2) : 420-5. doi : 10.4103/0976-9668. 117025.

3. The aim of the present study was to evaluate the anit-convulsant potential of
aqueous and ethanol extract of Glycyrrhiza glabra (AEGG and EEGG) and its
action on markers of oxidant stress in albino rats. The results demonstrate that
EEGG poses anti-convulsant potential and ameliorates ROS induced neuronal
damage in PTZ-induced seizure. Chowdhury B, Bhattamira SK, Das MC.
Anti-convulsant action and amelioration of oxidative stress by Glycyrrhiza
glabra root extract in pentylenetetrazole-induced seizure in albino rats. Inddian
J. Pharmacol. 2013 Jan-Feb; 45(1) : 40-3. doi : 10.4103/0253-7613.106433.

4. In this study, investigation done about the anti-obesity effect of glabridin and
glabridin rich supercritical fluid extract of licorice (LSC). Results suggest that
glabridin and glabridin-rich licorice exract would be effective anti-obesity
agents. Ahn J, Lee H, Jang J, Kim §, Ha T. Anti-obesity effects of glabridin-
rich supercritical carbon dioxide extract of licorice in high-fat-fed obese mice.
Food Chem Toxicol. 2013 Jan; 51:439-45. doi : 10.1016/j-fict. 2012. 08.048.
Epub 2012 Sep 4.

5. The present work was to study the effect of licorice aqueous extract on
carbendazim-induced testicualr toxicity in albino rats. The present results, it is
concluded that licorice aqueous extract can imporove the testicualr toxicity of
acarbendazim and this effect may be attributed to antioxidant porperties of one
or more of its constituents. Sakr S, Shalaby SY. Carbendazim-induced
testicular damage and oxidative stress in albino rats: Ameliorative effect of
licorice aqueous extract. Toxicol Ind Health. 2012 Aug 17.

Habitat

In India, Yashtimadhu is cultivated in Jammu Kashmir, Himachal Pradesh and Delhi.

Morphology

Habit -Tall perennial plant grows 50 cm to 1 mtr in height.

----- PAGE 728 -----
720 Dravyaguna Vijnana

Stem - Erect

Leaves — Compound, leaflets are 4 to 7 pairs ob-
long to elliptical-lanceolate, acute or obtuse.

Inflorescence — Raceme, shorter than the leaves.

Flowers — | cm long, bluish or violet coloured.
Calyx are 2 lipped, upper teeth connate higher than the

lower ones. Standard petal is narrow, wings and keel
are acute. Stamens are diadelphous. Ovary many ovuled.

Fruit — Pod, oblong to linear, 1 to 3 cm long, flat-
tened, straightish, more or less densely echinate glan-
dular many seeded. Glycyrrhiza glabra Linn.
Adulterants (Yastimadhu)

Roots of Glycyrrhiza uralensis Fisch and Abrus precatorius Linn. are often adulter-
ated with liquorice. Even stem pieces of Glycyrrhiza glabra is used as an adulterant.

Varieties
Dh. Ni. & Ra. Ni. - 2 types: 1. Yashtimadhu — 2. Klitanaka

Grahya Laxana (Genuine characteristics):

Market sample consists transversely cut pieces of dry clyindrical roots and stolons,
more often with a fibrous cut surface, 1 to 3 cm in diameter. External surface is shrunken,
rusty brown in colour, long tediously wrinkled with occasional small buds, circular root
scars and remnants of encircling leafy scales. Transversely cut surface shows a yellowish
brown fibrous structure depicting loose bark, followed by a well marked campus ring and
radiating vascular tissue. On breaking becomes fibrous, has got faint characteristic odour,
taste is sweet and mucilaginous.

Multiple Choice Questions (MCQ)

1. Useful part of Yashtimadhu
a. Stem b. Root and Stolon c. Fruit c. Seed
Ans: b. Root and Stolon

2. Pradhana Rasa of Yashtimadhu
a. Katu b. Tikta c. Madhura d. Kashaya
Ans: c. Madhura

3. Intake of rice milk processed with Yashtimadhu (Glycyrrhiza glabra) and ghee
is very useful
a. Jwara b. Swarabheda c. Ajima d. Kushtha
Ans: b. Swarabheda

4. Avapidaka (pressed snuff) Nasya with mixture of Yashtimadhu (Glycyrrhiza
glabra) and honey is good for
a. Kasa b. Pandu c. Hikka d. Ajirna
Ans: c. Hikka

5. Glycyrrhizine a chemical constituent present in
a. Vidari b. Manjishtha c. Vatsanabha d. Yashtimadhu

Ans: d. Yashtimadhu

----- PAGE 730 -----
722 Dravyaguna Vijnana

6. Yashtimadhuvadi Taila is specifically indicated in
a. Khalitya b. Vyanga c. Kushtha d. Udarashula
Ans: a. Khalitya
. Common English Name of Yashtimadhu
a. Nutmeg b. Marking Nut __c. Liquorice d. Flax seeds
Ans: c. Liquorice
8. Types of Yashtimadhu as per Dhanvantari Nighantu
a. Yashtimadhu & Klitanaka b. Yashtimadhu & Mahayashti
c. Yashtimadhu & Laghyashti d. Klitanaka & Maha Klitanaka
Ans: a. Yashtimadhu & Klitanaka
